International governments are divided on the issue of recognition of the independence of [Kosovo](17391.md) from [Serbia](29265.md), which was [declared](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Kosovo_declaration_of_independence) in 2008.[^cite_note-1][^cite_note-2] The [Government of Serbia](608371.md) does not diplomatically recognise Kosovo as a [sovereign state](23604120.md),[^cite_note-3] although the two countries have enjoyed normalised economic relations since 2020 and have agreed not to try to interfere with the other's accession to the [European Union](9317.md).

As of 19 December 2025, at least 110 out of 193 (57%) [United Nations](31769.md) member states, 22 out of 27 (81.5%) [European Union](9317.md) member states, 28 out of 32 (87.5%) [NATO](21133.md) member states and 37 out of 57 (64.9%) [Organisation of Islamic Cooperation](47725.md) member states have recognised [Kosovo](17391.md).[^cite_note-njohjeve-4] In total, Kosovo has received 110 diplomatic recognitions by UN member states, however conflicts have arisen regarding the exact number of countries recognising Kosovo. Kosovo claims that the number of countries recognising its independence is 120,[^cite_note-5] whilst Serbia claims the number is 84, stating that some countries have withdrawn recognition.[^cite_note-6] Third party sources give the number of recognising countries as around 110.[^cite_note-7][^cite_note-8][^cite_note-9]

Among the [G20](2206608.md) countries, eleven (including all seven [G7](1196634.md) countries) have recognised Kosovo as an independent state: [Australia](4689264.md), [Canada](5042916.md), [France](5843419.md), [Germany](11867.md), [Italy](14532.md), [Japan](15573.md), [Saudi Arabia](349303.md), [South Korea](27019.md), [Turkey](11125639.md), the [United Kingdom](31717.md), and the [United States](3434750.md). Eight (including all five founding [BRICS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BRICS) countries), however, have not recognised Kosovo as an independent state: [Argentina](18951905.md), [Brazil](3383.md), [China](5405.md), [India](14533.md), [Indonesia](14579.md), [Mexico](3966054.md), [Russia](25391.md), and [South Africa](17416221.md).

In 2013, the two sides began to normalise relations in accordance with the [Brussels Agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brussels_Agreement_\(2013\)). In September 2020, Serbia and Kosovo agreed to normalise economic ties.[^cite_note-APNews_KosovoSerbia-10][^cite_note-TheTimesofIsrael_KosovoSerbia-11] Serbia also agreed to suspend its efforts to encourage other states to either not recognise Kosovo or to revoke recognition for one year, while Kosovo agreed to not apply for new membership of international organisations for the same period.[^cite_note-Exit_KosovoSerbia-12] In February 2023, Serbia and Kosovo agreed to a proposed [normalisation agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agreement_on_the_path_to_normalisation_between_Kosovo_and_Serbia) in European Union mediated dialogue and through further negotiations accepted a roadmap and timescale for its implementation the following month. Under the terms of the agreement, Serbia committed to not oppose the membership of Kosovo in international organisations and recognised Kosovo's national symbols and official documents, including passports, diplomas, vehicle registration plates, and customs stamps.[^cite_note-EEAS2023-13][^cite_note-Fontelles2023-14][^cite_note-EEAS2023b-15]

## Background

Further information: [Foreign relations of Kosovo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Kosovo) and [Kosovo independence precedent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kosovo_independence_precedent)

A number of states expressed concern over the unilateral character of Kosovo's declaration, or explicitly announced that they would not recognise an independent Kosovo. The [United Nations Security Council](31956.md) (UNSC) remains divided on this issue: of its five members with [veto power](1900272.md), three (France, the United Kingdom, and the United States) have recognised the declaration of independence, while the People's Republic of China has expressed concern, urging the continuation of the previous negotiation framework. The Russian Federation (which has close ties with Serbia[^cite_note-turns_2023_09_25_politico-16]) has rejected the declaration and considers it illegal,[^cite_note-17] and does not recognise Kosovo's independence.[^cite_note-turns_2023_09_25_politico-16]

In May 2008, Russia, China, and India released a joint statement calling for new negotiations between [Belgrade](55904.md) and [Pristina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pristina).[^cite_note-Ekaterinburg2-18]

Although EU member states individually decide whether to recognise Kosovo, by consensus the EU has commissioned the [European Union Rule of Law Mission in Kosovo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Union_Rule_of_Law_Mission_in_Kosovo) (EULEX) to ensure peace and continued external oversight. Due to the dispute in the United Nations Security Council (UNSC), the reconfiguration of the [United Nations Interim Administration Mission in Kosovo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Interim_Administration_Mission_in_Kosovo) (UNMIK) and partial handover to the EULEX mission met with difficulties. Despite Russian and Serbian protests, the [UN Secretary-General](162415.md) [Ban Ki-moon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ban_Ki-moon) proceeded with the reconfiguration plan. On 15 July 2008, he stated: "In the light of the fact that the Security Council is unable to provide guidance, I have instructed my [Special Representative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_Representative_of_the_Secretary-General_for_Kosovo) to move forward with the reconfiguration of UNMIK ... in order to adapt UNMIK to a changed reality." According to the Secretary-General, the "United Nations has maintained a position of strict neutrality on the question of Kosovo's status".[^cite_note-19] On 26 November 2008, the UNSC gave the green light to the deployment of the EULEX mission in Kosovo. The EU mission is to assume police, justice, and customs duties from the UN, while operating under the [United Nations Security Council Resolution 1244](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_1244) (UNSCR 1244) that first placed Kosovo under UN administration in 1999.[^cite_note-20]



Recognition of Kosovo by UN member states over time (total members 193)

A [United Nations General Assembly](31957.md) (UNGA) resolution adopted on 8 October 2008 backed the request of Serbia to seek an [International Court of Justice advisory opinion on Kosovo's declaration of independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Advisory_opinion_on_Kosovo's_declaration_of_independence).[^cite_note-UN_Backing_Request_by_Serbia...-21] On 22 July 2010, the ICJ ruled that the declaration of independence of Kosovo did not violate [international law](8195726.md) "because international law contains no prohibition on declarations of independence", and that its authors were not bound by the [Constitutional Framework](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Provisional_Institutions_of_Self-Government) (promulgated by UNMIK) or by [UNSCR 1244](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_1244), that is addressed only to [United Nations Member States](31969.md) and [organs of the United Nations](216034.md).[^cite_note-International_Court_of_Justice-22][^cite_note-23]

Within the EU, key supporters of Kosovo's statehood include France[^cite_note-24] and Germany.[^cite_note-25] The strongest opponents to Kosovo's statehood within the EU include Spain[^cite_note-Ferrero-Turrión-26][^cite_note-Ferrero-Turrión2-27] and Greece.[^cite_note-Armakolas-28] The Spanish non-recognition of Kosovo is linked to the Spanish government's opposition to the [Basque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basque_nationalism) and [Catalan independence movements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catalan_independence_movement),[^cite_note-Ferrero-Turrión-26] while the Greek non-recognition of Kosovo is linked to the [Cyprus dispute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cyprus_dispute) and Greece's historic relationship to Serbia.[^cite_note-Armakolas-28]

## Serbia's reaction

Main articles: [Serbia's reaction to the 2008 Kosovo declaration of independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serbia's_reaction_to_the_2008_Kosovo_declaration_of_independence), [Kosovo–Serbia relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kosovo%E2%80%93Serbia_relations), and [Belgrade–Pristina negotiations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belgrade%E2%80%93Pristina_negotiations)

See also: [2008 protests against Kosovo declaration of independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_protests_against_Kosovo_declaration_of_independence)

Due to Serbian claims that Kosovo is part of its sovereign territory, its initial reactions included recalling ambassadors from countries that recognised Kosovo for several months, indicting Kosovar leaders on charges of high treason, and litigating the case at the International Court of Justice (ICJ). Serbia also expelled ambassadors from countries that recognised Kosovo after the UNGA vote adopting Serbia's initiative to seek an ICJ [advisory opinion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Advisory_opinion).[^cite_note-29]

In December 2012, as a result of [European Union](9317.md) mediated [negotiations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belgrade%E2%80%93Pristina_negotiations) on [Kosovo's status](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_status_of_Kosovo), Serbian Prime Minister [Ivica Dačić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ivica_Da%C4%8Di%C4%87) agreed to appoint a liaison officer to Kosovo.[^cite_note-30] In March 2013, Dačić said that while his government would never recognise Kosovo's independence, "the Serbian president cannot go to Kosovo, nor the prime minister, nor ministers, nor the police or army. Serbs can only leave Kosovo. That's how much Kosovo is ours and what our constitution and laws mean there".[^cite_note-31]

In April 2013, Kosovo and Serbia reached [an agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brussels_Agreement_\(2013\)) to normalise relations, and thereby allow both nations to eventually join the [European Union](9317.md).[^cite_note-32] On 17 June 2013 Kosovo and Serbia exchanged liaison officers.[^cite_note-33]

However, the process of normalisation stalled in November 2018, after which Kosovo imposed a 100 percent tax on importing Serbian goods. On 1 April 2020, Kosovo withdrew the tax.[^cite_note-34]

In September 2020, under an agreement brokered by the United States, Serbia and Kosovo agreed to normalise economic ties.[^cite_note-APNews_KosovoSerbia-10][^cite_note-TheTimesofIsrael_KosovoSerbia-11] Serbia also agreed to suspend its efforts to encourage other states to either not recognise Kosovo or to revoke recognition for one year, while Kosovo agreed to not apply for new membership of international organisations for the same period.[^cite_note-Exit_KosovoSerbia-12]

In February 2023, Serbia and Kosovo agreed to a proposed [normalisation agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agreement_on_the_path_to_normalisation_between_Kosovo_and_Serbia) in European Union-mediated dialogue and, through further negotiations, accepted a roadmap and timescale for its implementation the following month. Under the terms of the agreement, Serbia committed to not oppose the membership of Kosovo in international organisations and recognised Kosovo's national symbols and official documents, including passports, diplomas, vehicle registration plates, and customs stamps.[^cite_note-EEAS2023-13][^cite_note-Fontelles2023-14][^cite_note-EEAS2023b-15]

In December 2023, the head of Serbia's office for Kosovo, [Petar Petković](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petar_Petkovi%C4%87), announced that the Serbian Government had decided to enable the free movement of all vehicles with Kosovo plates into Serbian territory, starting from 1 January 2024.[^cite_note-35][^cite_note-36]

## International Court of Justice advisory opinion

Main article: [Advisory opinion on Kosovo's declaration of independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Advisory_opinion_on_Kosovo's_declaration_of_independence)

On 27 March 2008, Serbian Foreign Minister [Vuk Jeremić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vuk_Jeremi%C4%87) said Serbia would request the [International Court of Justice](14918.md) to review the legality of Kosovo's declaration of independence.[^cite_note-37] On 8 October 2008, the [UN General Assembly](31957.md) adopted Serbia's resolution,[^cite_note-38] with 77 votes in favour, 6 votes against and 74 abstentions.[^cite_note-39] The court delivered its opinion on 22 July 2010; by a vote of 10 to 4, it held that "the adoption of the declaration of independence of 17 February 2008 did not violate general [international law](8195726.md) because international law contains no 'prohibition on declarations of independence', nor did the adoption of the declaration of independence violate UN Security Council [Resolution 1244](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Resolution_1244), since this did not describe Kosovo's final status, nor had the Security Council reserved for itself the decision on final status.[^cite_note-40][^cite_note-41][^cite_note-42]

## Positions taken by UN member states and other entities

According to a 2020 study, states which have stronger ties to the United States are more likely to recognise Kosovo, whereas states with stronger ties to Russia are less likely to recognise Kosovo.[^cite_note-43]

| 58 | [Comoros](5403.md) | 14 May 2009 | [^cite_note-njohjeve-4][^cite_note-119][^cite_note-Koha-120] | Yes | OIC Further detailsRecognition withdrawn 1 November 2018.[^cite_note-121][^cite_note-122][^cite_note-123][^cite_note-124][^cite_note-125] In February 2021, the former Minister of Foreign Affairs of Kosovo, [Behgjet Pacolli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Behgjet_Pacolli), posted on the social network Facebook a letter from the Minister of Foreign Affairs of the Comoros. However, the letter states that "the Comorian authorities have taken note of Kosovo’s request to establish diplomatic relations with the Union of the Comoros. | 65 | [Eswatini](27451.md) | 12 April 2010 | [^cite_note-135] | No | Further detailsIn January 2023, Serbian President [Aleksandar Vučić](13349267.md) announced that Eswatini, amongst eight other nations, had withdrawn its recognition of Kosovo's independence.[^cite_note-nine-83] In the same month, Kosovo's Ambassador to the United Kingdom, Ilir Kapiti, met with Eswatini’s High Commissioner Thandazile P. Mbuyisa. According to Kapiti’s post, they "are looking forward to strengthening relations Eswatini-Kosovo by exploring economic opportunities".[^cite_note-136][^cite_note-debunk-137] In May 2023, State Secretary at the Ministry of Defence [Nemanja Starović](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nemanja_Starovi%C4%87) met Ambassador of the Kingdom of Eswatini, Sibusisive Mngomezulu. According to MD Serbia during meeting Starović expressed "his gratitude to Eswatini for withdrawing the recognition of unilaterally declared independence of Kosovo, and for the support for the territorial integrity and sovereignty of Serbia".[^cite_note-138] In September 2025, Minister of Defence [Bratislav Gašić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bratislav_Ga%C5%A1i%C4%87) met with Prince Sicalo Nkopolo Dlamini, Principal Secretary of the Ministry of National Defence and Security of the Kingdom of Eswatini. During the meeting, Gašić expressed "his gratitude for Eswatini’s support for Serbia’s territorial integrity and sovereignty, particularly in light of Eswatini’s decision to withdraw its recognition of independence of Kosovo".[^cite_note-139] |

| 68 | [Somalia](27358.md) | 19 May 2010 | [^cite_note-142] | Yes | OIC Further detailsIn January 2023, Serbian President [Aleksandar Vučić](13349267.md) announced that Somalia, amongst eight other nations, had withdrawn its recognition of Kosovo's independence.[^cite_note-nine-83] During the same month, the Embassy of Kosovo in Turkey reported that Kosovan Ambassador Agon Vrenezi met with his Somali counterpart Jama A. Mohamed, stating that they had "discussed the possibilities of deepening cooperation between the two countries".[^cite_note-143] In March 2023, Kosovo's president [Vjosa Osmani](61979540.md) met [Abshir Omar Huruse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abshir_Omar_Huruse), the [foreign minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_\(Somalia\)) of Somalia. According to Osmani's statement, both sides "reaffirmed our excellent bilateral relations".[^cite_note-144] In November 2024, during a joint press conference with Somalia’s Minister of Internal Security, Abdullahi Sheikh Ismail, Serbian Interior Minister [Ivica Dačić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ivica_Da%C4%8Di%C4%87) reiterated that Somalia had revoked its recognition of Kosovo, expressing gratitude for Somalia’s respect for Serbia’s territorial integrity.[^cite_note-145] According to the Tanjug news agency, in March 2025 Serbian Foreign Minister [Marko Đurić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marko_%C4%90uri%C4%87) held a telephone conversation with Somali Foreign Minister [Ahmed Moalim Fiqi](41451994.md), thanking Somalia for supporting Serbia’s sovereignty and territorial integrity. Đurić emphasised that "Serbia highly appreciates Somalia’s support regarding the autonomous province of Kosovo and Metohija".[^cite_note-146] In September 2025, it was claimed that Somalia had rejected recognition of Kosovo.[^cite_note-147][^cite_note-148] On 17 February 2026, Somalia’s President sent a letter to Kosovo’s President congratulating the Republic of Kosovo on the 18th anniversary of its independence confirming that Somalia continues to recognise Kosovo's independence. |

| 73 | [Guinea-Bissau](12186.md) | 10 January 2011 | [^cite_note-154] | Yes | OIC; Recognition withdrawn 21 November 2017,[^cite_note-155][^cite_note-156][^cite_note-157] withdrawal was annulled 2 February 2018.[^cite_note-GBRest-158][^cite_note-GBRest2-159] |
| 74 | [Oman](22316.md) | 4 February 2011 | [^cite_note-160][^cite_note-njohjeve-4][^cite_note-fm.gov.om-161] | Yes | OIC; [Kosovo–Oman relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kosovo%E2%80%93Oman_relations) Further detailsThere are conflicting reports on whether Oman has recognised Kosovo or not. On 4 February 2011, the MFA of Kosovo announced that it received a note from Oman which stated that it "will welcome Kosovo's membership to the United Nations, as well as to other international and regional organizations" and commented on it by the foreign minister [Vlora Çitaku](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vlora_%C3%87itaku) as recognition of independence and the establishment of diplomatic relations.[^cite_note-162][^cite_note-163] However, in September 2011 new Kosovo's deputy Foreign Minister [Petrit Selimi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petrit_Selimi) accepted that it had misread this as recognition and posted that "Oman had never recognized as Vlora (Çitaku) clarified in a statement in Feb. They sent warm letter of wishing us to enter UN".[^cite_note-164][^cite_note-165][^cite_note-Selimi,_Jeremic-166] In the same month Serbia's Foreign Minister [Vuk Jeremić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vuk_Jeremi%C4%87) announced that Oman never recognised Kosovo. He said that "a note from Oman has informed us that Oman’s position on Kosovo has not changed".[^cite_note-Selimi,_Jeremic-166] Later that month, Kosovo's MFA announced that Oman's Foreign Minister [Yusuf bin Alawi bin Abdullah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yusuf_bin_Alawi_bin_Abdullah) had informed them of his country's recognition of Kosovo.[^cite_note-167] But even this statement has been cast into doubt. In February 2012, Rexhep Boja, the chargé d’affaires at the Kosovo embassy in Saudi Arabia, stated that "five GCC states, including Saudi Arabia, have recognized our independence", adding that "Kosovo is still waiting for Oman to recognize it".[^cite_note-168][^cite_note-169] In September 2020, Kosovo’s Foreign Minister [Meliza Haradinaj-Stublla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meliza_Haradinaj-Stublla) held a videoconference with her Omani counterpart, [Sayyid Badr bin Hamad bin Hamood Al Busaidi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sayyid_Badr_bin_Hamad_bin_Hamood_Al_Busaidi). According to the MFA of Kosovo, Haradinaj-Stublla discussed with Al Busaidi "the need to deepen relations between Kosovo and Oman". But press release also confirms that "the Republic of Kosovo and Oman have not yet established diplomatic relations".[^cite_note-170] Then, three official meetings took place here between representatives of Kosovo and Oman, as reported by the Omani Ministry of Foreign Affairs. In September 2021, Kosovo’s President [Vjosa Osmani](61979540.md) and Foreign Minister [Donika Gërvalla-Schwarz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donika_G%C3%ABrvalla-Schwarz) held a videoconference with her Omani counterpart, [Sayyid Badr Albusaidi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sayyid_Badr_Albusaidi). During the meeting, the two sides "stressed the importance of working continuously to expand and diversify common interests in various fields". Omani MFA referred to Osmani and Gërvalla-Schwarz as the president and FM of the "Republic of Kosovo". In March 2024, Kosovo’s Foreign Minister [Donika Gërvalla-Schwarz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donika_G%C3%ABrvalla-Schwarz) met with Mohammed bin Awadh Alhassan, Oman’s Permanent Representative to the United Nations in New York. Gërvalla-Schwarz called for "strengthening the bonds of cooperation and friendship between Oman and Kosovo". In September 2025, Kosovo’s Prime Minister [Albin Kurti](4774301.md) met with Omani Foreign Minister [Sayyid Badr Albusaidi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sayyid_Badr_Albusaidi) in New York. Kurti expressed his country’s "interest in benefiting from Oman’s experience and in strengthening practical cooperation in the fields of education, tourism, and investment". The issues of Kosovo’s recognition and the establishment of diplomatic relations were not mentioned in the press releases.[^cite_note-fm.gov.om-161][^cite_note-171][^cite_note-172] |

Dačić told, that "we especially thank St. Lucia for its decision to withdraw its recognition of Kosovo".[^cite_note-179] In June 2025 [Alva Baptiste](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alva_Baptiste) met with Serbian new Foreign Minister [Marko Đurić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marko_%C4%90uri%C4%87). | 80 | [Gabon](12027.md) | 15 September 2011 | [^cite_note-181][^cite_note-182] | Yes | OIC Further detailsIn January 2023, Serbian President [Aleksandar Vučić](13349267.md) announced that Gabon, amongst eight other nations, had withdrawn its recognition of Kosovo's independence.[^cite_note-nine-83][^cite_note-183] In the same month, the Kosovan Ambassador to Canada, Adriatik Kryeziu, met with his counterpart from Gabon, Engone Rosine Epouse Oliveira. According to Kryeziu’s post they had "discussion on Kosovo-Gabon current bilateral relations and future cooperation".[^cite_note-184][^cite_note-debunk-137] In April 2023, during an official visit by the Gabonese Foreign Minister Yolande Nyonda, Serbian Defence Minister [Miloš Vučević](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milo%C5%A1_Vu%C4%8Devi%C4%87) expressed his gratitude to Gabon for withdrawing its recognition of Kosovo's independence.[^cite_note-185] In May 2024, a Gabonese government delegation paid an official visit to Serbia. According to the Ministry of Defence of Serbia, during the meeting between the defence ministers of Serbia and Gabon, Serbian Minister [Bratislav Gašić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bratislav_Ga%C5%A1i%C4%87) thanked Gabon for "supporting the territorial integrity and sovereignty of Serbia, as well as for withdrawing its recognition of the unilaterally declared independence of the so-called Kosovo".[^cite_note-186] Furthermore, after a meeting between the Prime Ministers of Serbia, [Miloš Vučević](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milo%C5%A1_Vu%C4%8Devi%C4%87), and Gabon, [Raymond Ndong Sima](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raymond_Ndong_Sima), it was stated at their joint press conference that Gabon had previously recognised Kosovo but now recognises the territorial integrity of Serbia.[^cite_note-187][^cite_note-188][^cite_note-189] |

| 83 | [Ghana](12067.md) | 23 January 2012 | [^cite_note-Ghana-193][^cite_note-194][^cite_note-195] | Yes | [Ghana–Kosovo relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghana%E2%80%93Kosovo_relations) Further detailsRecognition was withdrawn on 7 November 2019.[^cite_note-196][^cite_note-197][^cite_note-198] The 2022 Kosovo Diplomatic List in 2022 still stated that the Ghanaian embassy in Ankara, Turkey is accredited to the Republic of Kosovo with the Ghanaian ambassador in Ankara being described as "Ambassador Extraordinary and Plenipotentiary – Non resident in Pristina".[^cite_note-199] In April 2025, secretary of the former President of Kosovo Behgjet Pacolli, Jetlir Zyberaj, met the President of Ghana [John Mahama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Mahama). Đurić expressed "deep appreciation for Ghana’s consistent support of Serbia’s territorial integrity and sovereignty, emphasizing the principled stance Ghana maintains on this matter".[^cite_note-202][^cite_note-203][^cite_note-204] In September 2025, Albanian President of Kosovo [Bajram Begaj](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bajram_Begaj) met President of Ghana John Mahama. According to Bajram Begaj’s post he "met with Ghana’s President Mahama to discuss advancing our bilateral and multilateral cooperation, and confirming Ghana’s recognition for Kosova".[^cite_note-205][^cite_note-206] |

| 96 | [Grenada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grenada) | 25 September 2013 | [^cite_note-225][^cite_note-226][^cite_note-227] | Yes | Further detailsRecognition has been officially suspended 30 October 2018.[^cite_note-228][^cite_note-N1_Grenada-229] In March 2019, Serbia's Foreign Minister [Ivica Dačić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ivica_Da%C4%8Di%C4%87) met with his Grenadian counterpart [Charles Peter David](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peter_David_\(politician\)). During meeting, according to MFA Serbia, Djuric reiterated "Serbia’s appreciation for Grenada’s stance on the issue of the unilaterally declared independence of Kosovo", emphasising that "through its consistent position, Grenada has demonstrated a firm commitment to the core principles of international law and the Charter of the United Nations".[^cite_note-233] |

### Countries which have recognised Kosovo but have subsequently been reported to have withdrawn recognition

Some countries have recognised Kosovo, but later have been reported to have withdrawn their recognition. These are:

| No. | Country | Date of | Ref. | Relevant membership, further details |  |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Recognition | Withdrawal |  |  |  |  |
| 1 | [Burundi](21490998.md) | 16 October 2012 | 15 February 2018 | [^cite_note-270][^cite_note-271][^cite_note-272] |  |
| 2 | [Papua New Guinea](22943.md) | 3 October 2012 | 5 July 2018 | [^cite_note-273] |  |
| 3 | [Dominica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dominica) | 11 December 2012 | 2 November 2018 | [^cite_note-274][^cite_note-275][^cite_note-276][^cite_note-277] |  |
| 4 | [Solomon Islands](265083.md) | 13 August 2014 | 28 November 2018 | [^cite_note-278][^cite_note-279] |  |
| 5 | [Madagascar](18964.md) | 24 November 2017 | 7 December 2018 | [^cite_note-280] |  |
| 6 | [Togo](30138.md) | 11 July 2014 | 28 June 2019 | [^cite_note-281][^cite_note-282][^cite_note-283] | OIC Further details16 January 2023, Togo confirmed its withdrawal of recognition of Kosovo.[^cite_note-284] |
| 7 | [Central African Republic](5478.md) | 22 July 2011 | 24 July 2019 | [^cite_note-Central_African_Republic-285][^cite_note-286][^cite_note-287] |  |
| 8 | [Nauru](21302.md) | 23 April 2008 | 13 November 2019 | [^cite_note-Nauru-288][^cite_note-289][^cite_note-290] |  |

The Serbian Foreign Ministry claimed in March 2020 that a total of 18 countries had withdrawn their recognition: aside from the 8 listed above, Serbia also mentioned: Grenada, Guinea-Bissau, Liberia, Palau, São Tomé and Príncipe, and Suriname.[^cite_note-291][^cite_note-292][^cite_note-293] In some of those cases, Kosovo's foreign ministry has called it "[fake news](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fake_news)" and "Serbian propaganda".[^cite_note-294][^cite_note-295][^cite_note-296][^cite_note-297]

### Countries which have not recognised Kosovo as an independent state

[Diplomatic recognition](524626.md) is an explicit, official, unilateral act in the foreign policy of states regarding another party. Not having issued such a statement does not necessarily mean the state has objections to the existence, independence, sovereignty, or government of the other party. Some states, by custom or policy, do not extend formal recognition on the grounds that a vote for membership in the UN or another organisation whose membership is limited to states is itself an act of recognition.

#### Member states of the United Nations

##### A

| Country | Position |
| --- | --- |
| [Algeria](358.md) | Main article: [Algeria–Kosovo relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algeria%E2%80%93Kosovo_relations) In March 2008, [Mourad Medelci](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mourad_Medelci), Algerian Foreign Minister, stated that while Algeria sympathised with all [Muslim countries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muslim_world), it believed that international laws had to be adhered to.[^cite_note-298] A year later, Medelci reaffirmed the Algerian position of Kosovo being an integral part of Serbia.[^cite_note-299] |
| [Angola](701.md) | On 23 June 2008, Angolan president [José Eduardo dos Santos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Eduardo_dos_Santos) sent a message to his Serbian counterpart, [Boris Tadić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boris_Tadi%C4%87), regarding Kosovo's declaration of independence. It reiterated the solidarity of dos Santos and Angola with Serbia in regard to the preservation of its sovereignty and integrity.[^cite_note-300] |
| [Argentina](18951905.md) | Main article: [Argentina–Kosovo relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Argentina%E2%80%93Kosovo_relations) In February 2008, Argentine Foreign Minister, [Jorge Taiana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jorge_Taiana) said "if we were to recognize Kosovo, which has declared its independence unilaterally, without an agreement with Serbia, we would set a dangerous precedent that would seriously threaten our chances of a political settlement in the case of the [Falkland Islands](7550182.md)".[^cite_note-301] He said that Argentina will not recognise it because it "supports the principle of territorial integrity", and he stressed that [UNSCR 1244](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_1244) called for the mutual agreement of both parties to solve the dispute.[^cite_note-302] At the [International Court of Justice](14918.md), the Argentine delegation argued that Kosovo's declaration of independence "breaches the obligation to respect the territorial integrity of Serbia, the obligation of peaceful settlement of disputes and the principle of non-intervention", and that any solution "did not, and could not, abolish Serbia's sovereignty over Kosovo".[^cite_note-303] |
| [Armenia](10918072.md) | Main article: [Armenia's reaction to the 2008 Kosovo declaration of independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenia's_reaction_to_the_2008_Kosovo_declaration_of_independence) On 12 March 2008, Armenian president [Serzh Sargsyan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serzh_Sargsyan) stated that Armenia's possible recognition of Kosovo's independence would not strain [Armenia–Russia relations](13875647.md), but also noted that "Kosovo recognition issue needs serious discussion ... Armenia has always been an adherent to the right of nations to self-determination and in this aspect we welcome Kosovo's independence",[^cite_note-panarmenian-304] but in September 2008 Sargsyan stated that Armenia had not recognised Kosovo's independence because "Armenia can not recognize another entity in the same situation as long as it has not recognized the [Nagorno-Karabakh](1000530.md) Republic".[^cite_note-panarmenian-304][^cite_note-305] On 4 April 2011 Sargsyan said that Armenia would not recognise the independence of Kosovo against Serbia's interests.[^cite_note-306] On 20 December 2024, Armenia recognised Kosovo passports as valid travel documents, allowing Kosovo nationals to visit to the country.[^cite_note-307][^cite_note-308] On 3 May 2026, Kosovo's acting Prime Minister [Albin Kurti](4774301.md) visited Armenia and met with Prime Minister [Nikol Pashinyan](17897961.md), discussing bilateral co-operation.[^cite_note-309][^cite_note-310] |
| [Azerbaijan](746.md) | Main article: [Azerbaijan–Kosovo relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijan%E2%80%93Kosovo_relations) In February 2008, a spokesman of the Foreign Ministry of Azerbaijan, [Khazar Ibrahim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khazar_Ibrahim), said "We view this illegal act as being in contradiction with international law. Proceeding from this, Azerbaijan's position was clear: it does not recognise \[Kosovo's] independence".[^cite_note-311] Azerbaijan has also withdrawn peacekeepers from Kosovo.[^cite_note-312] In 2009, Azerbaijan said, regarding Kosovo, that entities that declare secession while violating the internal laws of the state can not be considered to be states, and that a fait accompli may not be accepted.[^cite_note-Aze_Blr-313] In May 2010, President of Azerbaijan [Ilham Aliyev](22765442.md) stated that the proclaimed independence of Kosovo was unilateral and an illegal move.[^cite_note-314] |

##### B

| Country | Position |
| --- | --- |
| [Belarus](3457.md) | Main article: [Belarus–Kosovo relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belarus%E2%80%93Kosovo_relations) In February 2008, Belarusian President [Alexander Lukashenko](156589.md) wrote in a letter to Serbian President Boris Tadić that "Belarus expresses its solidarity with the Serbians' intention to defend their sovereignty and territorial integrity".[^cite_note-315] The [National Assembly of Belarus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Assembly_of_Belarus) had issued a statement condemning the declaration of independence and encouraged all nations to call the move "illegal" under international law.[^cite_note-316][^cite_note-317] |
| [Bhutan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhutan) | On 19 September 2012, the representative of Bhutan to the UN, [Lhatu Wangchuk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lhatu_Wangchuk), said that his country was deliberating the issue of Kosovo.[^cite_note-318] |
| [Bolivia](3462.md) | Main article: [Bolivia–Kosovo relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bolivia%E2%80%93Kosovo_relations) In February 2008, Bolivian president [Evo Morales](284163.md) refused to recognise Kosovo's independence and compared Kosovo separatists to the leaders of [four eastern Bolivian states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Bolivian_autonomy_referendums) who had demanded greater autonomy from the national government.[^cite_note-venbol2-319] In a 4 December 2009 hearing at the ICJ, the Bolivian delegation said that Kosovo was an integral part of Serbia, that the Republic of Kosovo did not exist, and that a "unilateral declaration of independence cannot change the international regime established by the UNSC resolution, or decide the outcome of negotiations".[^cite_note-MSP,_dan_četvrti-320] |
| [Bosnia and Herzegovina](3463.md) | Main article: [Bosnia and Herzegovina–Kosovo relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bosnia_and_Herzegovina%E2%80%93Kosovo_relations) Bosnia and Herzegovina's reaction to Kosovo's independence has been mixed. [Bosniak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bosniaks) and [Croat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croats_of_Bosnia_and_Herzegovina) members of the [Presidency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidency_of_Bosnia_and_Herzegovina) want to recognise it, but [Serb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serbs_of_Bosnia_and_Herzegovina) members refuse to do so, citing the possibility of [secession](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proposed_secession_of_Republika_Srpska) of Bosnia's [Republika Srpska](347422.md) on the same grounds of national self-determination (the [Kosovo precedent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kosovo_precedent)).[^cite_note-321] |
| [Botswana](3464.md) | In October 2010, Botswana's foreign minister [Phandu Skelemani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phandu_Skelemani) said that Botswana had not yet made a decision on recognition of Kosovo pending a ruling of the [European Union](9317.md), and that Kosovo had promised support in the establishment of a medical school in return for its recognition.[^cite_note-322] |
| [Brazil](3383.md) | Main article: [Brazil–Kosovo relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazil%E2%80%93Kosovo_relations) Brazil has not recognised the independence of Kosovo, stating that any change should be reached under the auspices of the United Nations and the legal framework of [UNSCR 1244](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_1244). |

##### C

| Country | Position |
| --- | --- |
| [Cambodia](334751.md) | On 6 October 2008, the Europe Department Director at the [Ministry of Foreign Affairs and International Cooperation of Cambodia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_and_International_Cooperation_\(Cambodia\)), Kao Samreth, stressed that Cambodia does not wish to encourage secession in any country and therefore does not support the independence of Kosovo. Kao drew parallels to independence claims for [South Ossetia](318381.md) and stated that Cambodia would not encourage tension within a country by supporting independence claims.In February 2009, Secretary of State at Ministry of Foreign Affairs and International Cooperation of Cambodia, Ouch Borith, reiterated an earlier Cambodian Ministry of Foreign Affairs statement that Kosovo was a sensitive issue which they are studying carefully. Borith questioned, "if Kosovo is recognized, what about South Ossetia?" In April 2009, it was stated that Cambodia had no plans to file a brief (either supportive of Serbia or Kosovo) in the ICJ case. |
| [Cameroon](5447.md) | Main article: [Cameroon–Kosovo relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cameroon%E2%80%93Kosovo_relations) In January 2011, the General Secretary of Cameroon's Ministry of Foreign Affairs, Ferdinand Ngoh Ngoh, reportedly said that his government could not deny that Kosovo's independence was irreversible, but that it would have to be careful on how to proceed in order not to create a situation that would damage Cameroon's interests and position in the world. In 2018, Cameroon was one of 51 countries to vote against Kosovo's bid to joint [Interpol](14338129.md), effectively blocking it from becoming a member country of the organisation.[^cite_note-neighbors-323] |
| [Cape Verde](18962637.md) | In December 2010, Cape Verde's National Director of Political Affairs and Co-operation, José Luis Rocha, said that his country would wait until there was consensus at the [United Nations Security Council](31956.md) before considering its position.[^cite_note-324] |
| [Chile](5489.md) | In a 27 February 2008 press release, the [Foreign Ministry of Chile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_\(Chile\)) called on the parties concerned to achieve, by peaceable means, through dialogue and adherence to the international law, a solution that respects the principles and purposes of the [United Nations Charter](88805.md). Chile has declared that it would continue to analyse the discussions that have taken place both in the [United Nations Security Council](31956.md) and in the [Council of Ministers of the European Union](5866.md).[^cite_note-325] |
| [China](5405.md) | Main article: [China's reaction to the 2008 Kosovo declaration of independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China's_reaction_to_the_2008_Kosovo_declaration_of_independence) The People's Republic of China supports Serbia's position on Kosovo, seeing parallels to [its own claim over Taiwan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taiwan,_China).[^cite_note-326] |
| [DR Congo](76762.md) | Main article: [Democratic Republic of the Congo–Kosovo relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Republic_of_the_Congo%E2%80%93Kosovo_relations) |
| [Congo Republic](19599929.md) |  |
| [Cuba](5042481.md) | Main article: [Cuba–Kosovo relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuba%E2%80%93Kosovo_relations) On 29 February 2008, writing in his personal "Reflections of Fidel" column, which was published in the official newspaper of the [Communist Party of Cuba](38776970.md), *[Granma Internacional](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Granma_\(newspaper\))*, [Fidel Castro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fidel_Castro) accused [Javier Solana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Solana) of being the ideological father of Kosovo's "independence" (Castro's quotes) and, by doing so, of putting at risk the ethnic cohesion and the very state integrity of Spain or the UK, both of which experienced separatist movements of their own.[^cite_note-327] |
| [Cyprus](5593.md) | Main article: [Cyprus–Kosovo relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cyprus%E2%80%93Kosovo_relations) On 11 February 2008, the Cypriot Foreign Minister [Erato Kozakou-Marcoullis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erato_Kozakou-Marcoullis), stated that "Cyprus will never recognize a unilateral declaration of independence outside the U.N. framework, and in particular by side-stepping the role of the Security Council".[^cite_note-328] This stance was reiterated in October 2009 by the President of Cyprus [Dimitris Christofias](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dimitris_Christofias), who said Cyprus would not recognise Kosovo, even if all other EU members did so.[^cite_note-329] Cyprus recognises travel documents issued by Kosovo.[^cite_note-rf61-330] |

##### E

| Country | Position |
| --- | --- |
| [Ecuador](9334.md) | Main article: [Ecuador–Kosovo relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ecuador%E2%80%93Kosovo_relations) In response to a request from the [University of Oxford](31797.md) regarding the analysis of developments related to the independence of Kosovo, in August 2008 the Ecuadorian Foreign Ministry stated that there should be "unrestricted compliance with the rules and principles of the [United Nations Charter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Charter) and [International Law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Law)".[^cite_note-331] |
| [Equatorial Guinea](9366.md) | On 1 September 2010, Equatorial Guinea's Permanent Representative to the UN, Anatolio Ndong Mba, said during a press conference that his country's foreign policy favours Kosovo's independence.[^cite_note-332] In September 2011, the President of Equatorial Guinea, [Teodoro Obiang Nguema Mbasogo](263622.md), is reported to have responded positively to a request for recognition by Kosovo.[^cite_note-333] During a visit of [Teodoro Nguema Obiang Mangue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Teodoro_Nguema_Obiang_Mangue) to Belgrade on 8 June 2022, it was stated that Equatorial Guinea has not changed its position about not recognising Kosovo.[^cite_note-334] |
| [Eritrea](17238590.md) | On 4 September 2008, the Director of the Euro-Americas Division at the [Eritrean Ministry of Foreign Affairs](34015067.md), Tsehaye Fassil, said that his Government had not decided whether it would recognise Kosovo's independence. |
| [Ethiopia](187749.md) | Main article: [Ethiopia–Kosovo relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethiopia%E2%80%93Kosovo_relations) In January 2009, Ethiopia's Ambassador to Austria reportedly stated that the Ethiopian government would decide on the recognition of Kosovo "at the right time".[^cite_note-335] According to Serbia, [Skënder Hyseni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sk%C3%ABnder_Hyseni), Kosovo's Foreign Minister, and other members of his delegation were denied entry into Ethiopia in January 2010. They allegedly wanted to attend an [African Union](10634933.md) summit in order to lobby African nations to recognise Kosovo. The Serbian Foreign Minister, [Vuk Jeremić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vuk_Jeremi%C4%87), said that their visas were denied after pressure from the Serbian government. Jeremić, who attended the summit, thanked his Ethiopian counterpart for denying the visas and supporting Serbia's cause. However, [Kosovo's Foreign Ministry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_\(Kosovo\)) denies that they submitted any requests for visas.[^cite_note-336] In December 2014, amid a diplomatic dispute with Serbia, Ethiopia threatened to recognise Kosovo.[^cite_note-337] |

##### G

| Country | Position |
| --- | --- |
| [Georgia](48768.md) | Main article: [Georgia–Kosovo relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Georgia%E2%80%93Kosovo_relations) The Foreign Minister of Georgia, [David Bakradze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Bakradze), said on 18 February 2008 that [Tbilisi](42298.md) would not recognise Kosovo's independence.[^cite_note-xinhua-338][^cite_note-339][^cite_note-340] On 29 March 2008 the prime minister, [Lado Gurgenidze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lado_Gurgenidze), gave a recorded interview in Estonia, in which he clearly said in English that as Georgia's friends have recognised Kosovo, it was only natural that eventually Georgia would do likewise. The printed publication of the interview elicited demands by the opposition to impeach him, and the government spokesman stated that the prime minister had been misinterpreted, after which the Estonian paper *[Postimees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Postimees)*, which conducted and printed the interview, released the audio publicly.[^cite_note-341] |
| [Greece](12108.md) | Main article: [Greece's reaction to the 2008 Kosovo declaration of independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greece's_reaction_to_the_2008_Kosovo_declaration_of_independence) As of 2022, Greece does not recognise the independence of Kosovo. In 2018, Greece was one of 51 countries that voted against Kosovo's bid to join [Interpol](14338129.md).[^cite_note-neighbors-323] Greece recognises travel documents issued by Kosovo.[^cite_note-rf61-330] |
| [Guatemala](17238567.md) | Main article: [Guatemala–Kosovo relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guatemala%E2%80%93Kosovo_relations) In March 2008, the Guatemalan Foreign Minister, [Haroldo Rodas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haroldo_Rodas), had said that he had objected to the recognition of Kosovo in deference to Russian concerns.At a meeting on 26 March 2009 with Kosovo's Foreign Minister, Skënder Hyseni, the Ambassador of Guatemala to the UN, [Gert Rosenthal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gert_Rosenthal), said that his country's government was carefully studying the developments in Kosovo, and the ongoing preparations to present a case to the ICJ. He also said that Guatemala was working with others in Latin America to reach a decision.[^cite_note-342] |

| [Iraq](7515928.md) | Main article: [Iraq–Kosovo relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iraq%E2%80%93Kosovo_relations) At a meeting on 28 May 2009 with Kosovo's Foreign Minister, Skënder Hyseni, the representative of Iraq to the UN, Hamid Al Bayati, reportedly said that Kosovo deserves to be recognised by other states and that Iraq's decision to recognise would come at a suitable time.[^cite_note-344] |

##### J

| Country | Position |
| --- | --- |
| [Jamaica](15660.md) | Main article: [Jamaica–Kosovo relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jamaica%E2%80%93Kosovo_relations) The Jamaican Government in 2009 refused a request from the United States to recognise Kosovo. On 23 July 2009, the Under Secretary for Multilateral Affairs at the [Jamaican Ministry of Foreign Affairs and Foreign Trade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_and_Foreign_Trade_\(Jamaica\)), Ambassador Vilma McNish, indicated that she expected no change in the government of Jamaica's decision not to extend formal diplomatic recognition to Kosovo.[^cite_note-345]Following April 2010 meetings with Jamaican officials, Serbian Foreign Minister [Vuk Jeremić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vuk_Jeremi%C4%87) stated that Serbia could count on Jamaica's continued support in the "preservation of its sovereignty and territorial integrity".[^cite_note-346]On 20 February 2020, the president of Kosovo, [Hashim Thaçi](3626980.md), stated on Twitter thanking Jamaica for recognising Kosovo as a sovereign and independent country. However, this was denied by Jamaica's Minister of Foreign Affairs and Foreign Trade [Kamina Johnson-Smith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamina_Johnson-Smith) the same day.[^cite_note-347][^cite_note-348][^cite_note-349] |

##### K

| Country | Position |
| --- | --- |
| [Kazakhstan](16642.md) | Kazakhstan has [opposed](#K) the independence of [Kosovo](17391.md) and has taken Serbia's position on the issue. In December 2008, Kazakh Prime Minister [Karim Masimov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karim_Masimov) stated that "We have an official position. Kazakhstan did not recognise Kosovo and does not recognise Abkhazia and South Ossetia. We consider that borders are defined and Kazakhstan will not recognise any new states".[^cite_note-350]In 2022, during a public discussion at the [St. Petersburg International Economic Forum](26267797.md), Kazakh president [Kassym-Jomart Tokayev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kassym-Jomart_Tokayev), while stating that Kazakhstan would not recognise the independence of breakaway states of [Donetsk](42425747.md) and [Luhansk People's Republics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luhansk_People's_Republic) in [Eastern Ukraine](13065940.md), also stated that Kazakhstan will also not recognise the independence of Kosovo and other [states with limited recognition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_states_with_limited_recognition#States_that_are_neither_UN_members_nor_UN_observers), claiming that "if the right to self-determination is put into practice all over the world, then there would be chaos."[^cite_note-351] |
| [North Korea](21255.md) | In March 2017, [North Korean](21255.md) Ambassador Ri Pyong Du visited [Belgrade](55904.md) and affirmed North Korean support for Serbia's territorial integrity.[^cite_note-352] |
| [Kyrgyzstan](170131.md) | In February 2008, a statement issued by [Kyrgyz Foreign Ministry](18387673.md) stated that Kyrgyzstan would not recognise Kosovo's independence and considered it a dangerous precedent for separatist organisations in the world.[^cite_note-353][^cite_note-354] |
