| [Lebanon](17771.md) | Main article: [Kosovo–Lebanon relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kosovo%E2%80%93Lebanon_relations)On 29 November 2018, Lebanese Foreign Minister [Gebran Bassil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gebran_Bassil) visited [Belgrade](55904.md) and affirmed in a joint press conference with his Serbian counterpart, [Ivica Dačić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ivica_Da%C4%8Di%C4%87), that Lebanon supported "the sovereignty and territorial integrity" of Serbia.[^cite_note-356][^cite_note-357] |

##### M

| Country | Position |
| --- | --- |
| [Mali](19127.md) | Main article: [Kosovo–Mali relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kosovo%E2%80%93Mali_relations)Following the August 2012 publication of a [note verbale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Note_verbale) recognising Kosovo's independence, purportedly signed by acting [President of Mali](430390.md), [Dioncounda Traoré](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dioncounda_Traor%C3%A9),[^cite_note-358] state run media in Mali issued a statement in which the Presidency of Mali denied recognising Kosovo and claimed that the document was a fabrication.[^cite_note-359] Pacolli claimed that the [Malian Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_of_Mali), who had recently seized control of the state in a [coup d'état](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_Malian_coup_d'%C3%A9tat) due to dissatisfaction over the government's handling of their own [separatist uprising](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tuareg_rebellion_\(2012\)) in [Azawad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azawad), had intervened to reverse the recognition granted by the civilian president.[^cite_note-360] On Pacolli's return to Mali to seek clarification on the issue, state leaders promised to reconfirm their recognition.[^cite_note-MALITOGO-361] |
| [Mauritius](19201.md) | On 8 May 2008, Mauritian Secretary General for Foreign Affairs, Anand Neewoor, stated that the Government of Mauritius would not recognise Kosovo any time soon because of their concerns that it would have implications for their "fight to regain the [Chagos Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chagos_Archipelago)". In June 2009, the Mauritian prime minister, [Navin Ramgoolam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Navin_Ramgoolam) reportedly called the US embassy in Port Louis to say that he had decided that Mauritius would recognise Kosovo, despite opposition from his foreign ministry. However, the recognition did not take place. |
| [Mexico](3966054.md) | On 19 February 2008, the [Mexican Secretariat of Foreign Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secretariat_of_Foreign_Affairs_\(Mexico\)) issued a statement saying that Mexico was closely paying attention to the situation as it develops in order to adopt, at an opportune moment, a position on the declaration of independence. The same statement called on all parties to agree peacefully, through dialogue, on the final status of Kosovo and to reach an agreement on the rights of minorities and the maintenance of peace and security in the Balkans.[^cite_note-362] Mexican government officials have since then said that Mexico does not intend to recognise Kosovo.[^cite_note-363] |
| [Moldova](19260.md) | Kosovo's declaration creates "deep concerns in the Republic of Moldova," the Moldovan government said in a February 2008 statement, and that Moldova would not recognise Kosovo's independence.[^cite_note-364] In December 2013, Moldovan Defence Minister [Vitalie Marinuța](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vitalie_Marinu%C8%9Ba) stated on a visit to Serbia that Moldova will not recognise Kosovo.[^cite_note-365] In the lead up to the [2nd summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2nd_European_Political_Community_Summit) of the [European Political Community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Political_Community) which Moldova is to host on 1 June 2023, legislation has been progressed in the Moldovan parliament that if enacted will recognise Kosovar passports as valid travel documents allowing holders to apply for [electronic visas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electronic_visa) to enter Moldova.[^cite_note-366] |
| [Mongolia](19271.md) | Main article: [Kosovo–Mongolia relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kosovo%E2%80%93Mongolia_relations)On 8 May 2009, Kosovo's president [Fatmir Sejdiu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fatmir_Sejdiu) met Nyamaa Enkhbold, the Mongolian Deputy Parliament Speaker, to request recognition of Kosovo by Mongolia. Enkhbold reportedly promised to deal with the request once he had returned home.[^cite_note-367] In a 17 July 2012 meeting with Kosovo's deputy prime minister, [Edita Tahiri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edita_Tahiri), both the Mongolian president and the Minister of Foreign Affairs and Trade, [Tsakhiagiin Elbegdorj](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tsakhiagiin_Elbegdorj) and [Gombojav Zandanshatar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gombojav_Zandanshatar), said that Mongolia would consider recognising the independence of Kosovo.[^cite_note-368] |
| [Morocco](19291.md) | Main article: [Kosovo–Morocco relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kosovo%E2%80%93Morocco_relations)In June 2011, [Moroccan government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moroccan_government) representatives explained that they had difficulty in recognising Kosovo due to the political context of [Western Sahara](33220.md).[^cite_note-369] |
| [Mozambique](19301.md) | Main article: [Kosovo–Mozambique relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kosovo%E2%80%93Mozambique_relations)In February 2008, Mozambican Deputy Foreign Minister Henrique Banze said in reference to Kosovo's declaration of independence, "We shall wait for the appropriate moment. It's a very sensitive matter and like all matters of this kind, it demands a lot of thought. Our government will work so that it may make the most appropriate decision in this case".[^cite_note-370] In September 2012, Mozambique's Minister for Foreign Affairs and Co-Operation, [Oldemiro Julio Marques Baloi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oldemiro_Julio_Marques_Baloi), said that his government would reconsider recognising Kosovo.[^cite_note-371] In February 2025, President of Kosovo, Vjosa Osmani, met with the Prime Minister of Mozambique, [Maria Benvinda Levy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maria_Benvinda_Levy), where they discussed common challenges and opportunities to strengthen co-operation between the two countries.[^cite_note-372] |
| [Myanmar](19457.md) | In January 2014, it was reported that Myanmar's Ministry of Foreign Affairs had sent a note to officials in Kosovo informing them that they had recognised Kosovo's independence. However, Pacolli and Hoxhaj quickly denied that they had received any such note.[^cite_note-373] |

##### N

| Country | Position |
| --- | --- |
| [Namibia](21292.md) | In September 2010, following talks with Namibian officials, Serbian Foreign Minister Vuk Jeremić stated that Namibia has no intentions of recognising Kosovo. The Foreign Minister of Namibia [Utoni Nujoma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Utoni_Nujoma) said that the most important thing was to continue to search for a peaceful solution to the problem of Kosovo and that opportunities should be sought for reconciliation between nations in the Balkans.[^cite_note-374] |
| [Nepal](171166.md) | A leaked 2009 cable from the US embassy in Kathmandu states that during a meeting with US Assistant Secretary [Richard Boucher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Boucher), the Foreign Secretary of Nepal, [Gyan Chandra Acharya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gyan_Chandra_Acharya), said that the Government of Nepal had yet to decide if it would recognise the independence of Kosovo. Acharya acknowledged that Nepal understood the US interest in Kosovo's recognition but could not make a decision at the time because of regional sensitivities. |
| [Nicaragua](21362.md) | In February 2008, Nicaragua's Foreign Minister, [Samuel Santos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samuel_Santos_L%C3%B3pez), said that his country maintained a position of "observation" to the unilateral declaration of independence of Kosovo. "Nicaragua is watching the issue of Kosovo's independence, we have friends who are in agreement with this independence and other friends who disagree, there are some who are saying that \[independence] is a threat to peace in that tender area. We just look at \[this case and] we have no opinion on this issue."[^cite_note-375] |
| [Nigeria](21383.md) | Main article: [Nigeria's reaction to the 2008 Kosovo declaration of independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nigeria's_reaction_to_the_2008_Kosovo_declaration_of_independence)In July 2009, [Umaru Yar'Adua](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Umaru_Yar'Adua), President of Nigeria, said that Nigeria will not recognise Kosovo as an independent nation. He said the decision not to recognise Kosovo was informed by Nigeria's historical experience of the [civil war of 1967 to 1970](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nigerian_Civil_War).[^cite_note-376] |

##### P

| Country | Position |
| --- | --- |
| [Paraguay](23420.md) | In February 2008, the Ministry of Foreign Relations of Paraguay published a statement saying that Paraguay took note of the independence declaration and was analysing the situation.[^cite_note-377]In April 2010, it was reported that the Paraguayan president had told Kosovan pilot James Berisha, who was on an awareness-raising journey around Central and South America, that Paraguay had already recognised Kosovo's independence but had not made this known so as not to jeopardise their relationship with Russia.[^cite_note-378] |
| [Philippines](23440.md) | In February 2008, Foreign Affairs Secretary [Alberto Romulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alberto_Romulo) said in a statement: "Considering the existing sensibilities in the region, continued dialogue should be encouraged among all the parties concerned to ensure regional stability". He also said the Philippines was not willing to recognise Kosovo as an independent nation.[^cite_note-379] On 19 February 2008, Romulo stated that recognition could complicate peace talks with [Muslim separatists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moro_people) in [Mindanao](20546.md). He said that "while the Philippines does not oppose the idea of independence for Kosovo, it would prefer a settlement ... taking into account the internationally accepted principles of sovereignty and territorial integrity".[^cite_note-380]In a November 2012 meeting with Kosovo's Foreign Minister, Enver Hoxhaj, Foreign Affairs Undersecretary, Rafael E. Seguis, pledged support for Kosovo, saying that his country has full understanding for the independence of Kosovo and that he would consider the request for recognition.[^cite_note-PHTH-381] |

##### R

| Country | Position |
| --- | --- |
| [Romania](25445.md) | Main article: [Romania's reaction to the 2008 Kosovo declaration of independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romania's_reaction_to_the_2008_Kosovo_declaration_of_independence) Romania has historically maintained a close relation with Serbia, which [it borders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romania%E2%80%93Serbia_border) for 546 kilometres. While this is an important factor, the main reason Romania does not recognise the independence of Kosovo remains the [Székely Land](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sz%C3%A9kely_Land) problem, Székely Land being a mostly [Hungarian inhabited](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hungarians_in_Romania) zone of Romania that has historically called for autonomy within Romania.On 18 February 2008, a joining sitting in the [Parliament of Romania](4462971.md) firmly [voted against the recognition of Kosovo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romania's_reaction_to_the_2008_Kosovo_declaration_of_independence), with 357 votes against recognising and only 27 votes for recognising. All the major parties, excluding the [Democratic Alliance of Hungarians in Romania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Alliance_of_Hungarians_in_Romania) (UDMR), opposed recognition, as well as the president [Traian Băsescu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Traian_B%C4%83sescu) and prime-minister [Călin Popescu-Tăriceanu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C%C4%83lin_Popescu-T%C4%83riceanu).[^cite_note-382][^cite_note-383]A potential reversion of the 2008 decision came in 2015, when Prime Minister [Victor Ponta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victor_Ponta) highlighted the changes that occurred in both Kosovo and Romania over the past years and hinted that Romania may follow the general position of the [European Union](9317.md) regarding Kosovo. Romania is one of the five countries that are members of the European Union but do not recognise Kosovo.[^cite_note-384]While official relations do not exist, Romania maintains a liaison office in Pristina. Kosovar citizens [can travel to Romania visa-free](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Visa_requirements_for_Kosovar_citizens#Visa_requirements) and Romania recognises the [Kosovar passport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kosovo_passport) as [legitimate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kosovo_passport#Recognition_of_the_passport_while_not_recognising_Kosovo_as_a_country).[^cite_note-rf61-330] |
| [Russia](25391.md) | Main article: [Russia's reaction to the 2008 Kosovo declaration of independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russia's_reaction_to_the_2008_Kosovo_declaration_of_independence)Russia has strongly opposed Kosovo's independence. But in 2014, when it recognised the [Declaration of Independence of the Republic of Crimea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Declaration_of_Independence_of_the_Republic_of_Crimea), Russia's Ministry of Foreign Affairs cited Kosovo's declaration and the ICJ decision as evidence that unilateral declarations of independence are not inconsistent with international law[^cite_note-385] (the [Kosovo independence precedent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kosovo_independence_precedent)). |
| [Rwanda](25645.md) | On 11 February 2009, the Director of International Organisations at Rwanda's Foreign Ministry, Ben Rutsinga, said that the [African Union](10634933.md) had no unified position on Kosovo independence and that Rwanda would not reach an "individual determination" in advance of such a unified position. On 18 September 2009, the Rwandan Foreign Minister, [Rosemary Museminali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rosemary_Museminali), said that some countries would be likely to criticise a Rwandan recognition of Kosovo, accusing Rwanda of taking that stance in order to lay the basis for a similar breakaway by parts of eastern Democratic Republic of the Congo. She added that the [Government of Kosovo](15826444.md) had requested a meeting with her, but she had not responded. |

##### S

| Country | Position |
| --- | --- |
| [Saint Vincent and the Grenadines](27228.md) | In February 2008, when asked about Kosovo, the Prime Minister of St. Vincent & the Grenadines, [Ralph Gonsalves](393744.md), said that "if the people of a country want independence, then I think they should have it".At a meeting in August 2011 with U.S. Representative, [Eliot Engel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eliot_Engel), both St. Vincent & the Grenadines' Prime Minister and Foreign Minister, Ralph Gonsalves and [Louis Straker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louis_Straker), took on board the case for recognition of Kosovo and promised to review the issue.[^cite_note-386] |
| [São Tomé and Príncipe](20026306.md) | In March 2012, São Tomé and Príncipe's Council of Ministers under then Prime Minister [Patrice Trovoada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patrice_Trovoada) adopted a resolution recognising Kosovo's independence.[^cite_note-387] In January 2013, President [Manuel Pinto da Costa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manuel_Pinto_da_Costa) issued a communication stating that the recognition was invalid as he had not been consulted on the decision, as required by the country's Constitution.[^cite_note-388][^cite_note-389][^cite_note-390][^cite_note-391] New Prime Minister [Gabriel Costa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gabriel_Costa_\(politician\)) said that the process of recognition was an anomalous situation.[^cite_note-392] Kosovo Foreign Minister, Enver Hoxhaj, insisted that the recognition remains valid.[^cite_note-balkaninsight.com-393] First Deputy Prime Minister of Kosovo [Behgjet Pacolli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Behgjet_Pacolli) said that he received a [note verbale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Note_verbale) recognising Kosovo from Trovoada and that it was a closed issue.[^cite_note-394] |
| [Serbia](29265.md) | *[See above](#Serbia's_reaction)* |
| [Seychelles](27288.md) | In September 2012, the Seychelles' Foreign Minister, Jean-Paul Adam, said that his country was not against Kosovo's independence, and that formal recognition would occur very soon.[^cite_note-395][^cite_note-396]In September 2014, Adam said that Seychelles would consider the recognition of Kosovo with utmost seriousness.[^cite_note-397] |
| [Slovakia](26830.md) | Main article: [Slovakia's reaction to the 2008 Kosovo declaration of independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slovakia's_reaction_to_the_2008_Kosovo_declaration_of_independence)Slovakia has not recognised Kosovo, but has given indications that its stance could change in the future, especially if independence will be agreed with Serbia. Slovakia recognises travel documents issued by Kosovo.[^cite_note-rf61-330] |
| [South Africa](17416221.md) | Main article: [South Africa's reaction to the 2008 Kosovo declaration of independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa's_reaction_to_the_2008_Kosovo_declaration_of_independence) South Africa's reaction to the independence of Kosovo has been mixed. |
| [South Sudan](32350676.md) | Main article: [Kosovo–South Sudan relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kosovo%E2%80%93South_Sudan_relations) In July 2011, Kosovo's First deputy prime minister, Behgjet Pacolli, was invited to attend South Sudan's independence ceremony.[^cite_note-398]In September 2012, South Sudan's vice-president, [Riek Machar](2407248.md) Teny, invited Kosovo's prime minister, [Hashim Thaçi](3626980.md), to South Sudan to discuss building bilateral relations between the two countries.[^cite_note-399] During an October 2012 meeting with Pacolli, South Sudan's president [Salva Kiir Mayardit](2363203.md) stated his country's desire to maintain friendly relations with Kosovo. He reiterated the position that South Sudan supports the right of the citizens of Kosovo to build and consolidate their state.[^cite_note-400] In September 2013, the Foreign Minister of South Sudan, [Barnaba Marial Benjamin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barnaba_Marial_Benjamin), confirmed that the recognition of Kosovo was a matter of time.[^cite_note-Botswana_South_Sudan-401]In April 2014, Benjamin said that positive news regarding improving relations with Kosovo should be expected.[^cite_note-Eri_SSD-402] In September 2014, Benjamin said that South Sudan was considering with seriousness the recognition of the independence and would follow all the procedures to do so.[^cite_note-403]On 2 March 2024, Kosovo's president [Vjosa Osmani](61979540.md) met South Sudanese foreign minister [James Pitia Morgan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Pitia_Morgan) and discussed the strengthening of co-operation between the two countries.[^cite_note-404] A statement from the South Sudanese foreign ministry referred to Osmani as the "President of the Republic of Kosovo" implying acknowledgement of the Republic of Kosovo as a state.[^cite_note-405] |
| [Spain](26667.md) | Main article: [Spain's reaction to the 2008 Kosovo declaration of independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spain's_reaction_to_the_2008_Kosovo_declaration_of_independence) Spain has been the only major country in Western Europe that has not recognised Kosovo, originally because of objections to the legality of its unilateral declaration of independence under international law, and also due to concerns about possible implications regarding its own issues with domestic independence movements.[^cite_note-Ferrero-Turrión2-27] In 2022, Spanish Prime Minister [Pedro Sánchez](43297512.md) reaffirmed Spain's nonrecognition of Kosovo, stating that their declaration of independence violated international law.[^cite_note-406] In January 2024 with the liberalisation of visas for Kosovo passports in the Schengen area, Spain now recognises Kosovar passports.[^cite_note-407] |
| [Sri Lanka](26750.md) | Main article: [Kosovo–Sri Lanka relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kosovo%E2%80%93Sri_Lanka_relations)In February 2008, the [Foreign Ministry of Sri Lanka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_\(Sri_Lanka\)) called Kosovo's declaration of independence a violation of the [UN Charter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN_Charter) and emphasised its concern that the act "could set an unmanageable precedent in the conduct of international relations, the established global order of sovereign States and could thus pose a grave threat to international peace and security".[^cite_note-408] |

##### T

| Country | Position |
| --- | --- |
| [Tajikistan](30108.md) | In February 2008, the Chairman of the International Affairs Committee of the [Tajik Assembly of Representatives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Assembly_of_Tajikistan), Asomudin Saidov, stated that Tajikistan will not recognise Kosovo's independence as it considers it to be a violation of legal norms and a danger for Europe.[^cite_note-409] According to leaked US cables, Tajikistan does not want to take a position on Kosovo due to concerns with the precedent for [Abkhazia](18933375.md) and [South Ossetia](318381.md). |
| [Trinidad and Tobago](3565457.md) | On 20 February 2008, Trinidad and Tobago's Foreign Minister [Paula Gopee-Scoon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paula_Gopee-Scoon) took a positive stance and promised the US Ambassador that she would pursue the matter of Kosovo's recognition.At a meeting on 25 March 2009 with Kosovo's Foreign Minister, Skënder Hyseni, the Ambassador of Trinidad and Tobago to the UN, Maria Annette Valere, said that her country knows how important the process of international recognition is for Kosovo, and that the government of Trinidad and Tobago would address the request for recognition in the near future.[^cite_note-410] |
| [Tunisia](30188.md) | Main article: [Kosovo–Tunisia relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kosovo%E2%80%93Tunisia_relations)At a meeting in October 2012 with Albanian Foreign Minister and Chairman of the Committee of Ministers of the Council of Europe, [Edmond Panariti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edmond_Panariti), the Tunisian Foreign Minister, [Rafik Abdessalem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafik_Abdessalem), said that his government was seriously considering the issue of the recognition of Kosovo.[^cite_note-411] |
| [Turkmenistan](198149.md) | In a September 2010 meeting with Albanian Prime Minister [Sali Berisha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sali_Berisha), Turkmen president [Gurbanguly Berdimuhamedow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gurbanguly_Berdimuhamedow) said that his country would consider the recognition of Kosovo at the right time.[^cite_note-412] |

##### U

| Country | Position |
| --- | --- |
| [Uganda](31816.md) | Main article: [Kosovo–Uganda relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kosovo%E2%80%93Uganda_relations) In February 2008, a senior Ugandan official said that the Ugandan government was carefully studying Kosovo's declaration of independence before it makes a decision to recognise it as a state or not.[^cite_note-Xinhuanet20080218-413][^cite_note-414]On 30 July 2023, President of Uganda [Yoweri Museveni](293409.md) visited Belgrade and was thanked by his Serbian counterpart for his support for Serbian territorial integrity.[^cite_note-415] |
| [Ukraine](31750.md) | Main article: [Ukraine's reaction to the 2008 Kosovo declaration of independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukraine's_reaction_to_the_2008_Kosovo_declaration_of_independence) Ukraine has, as of August 2020, not recognised the independence of Kosovo. On 6 August 2022, a resolution was presented in the [Verkhovna Rada](844993.md) by the [European Solidarity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Solidarity) party, which would recognise Kosovo as an independent state if passed.[^cite_note-416][^cite_note-417] |
| [Uruguay](31843.md) | According to *Últimas Noticias*, in March 2008, "Uruguay has not recognised Kosovo's declaration of independence, because doing so would not be in accordance with its required three pillars of recognition: the principle of territorial integrity of states, achieving a solution through dialogue and consensus, and recognition by international organisations."[^cite_note-418]On 27 September 2010, Uruguayan Deputy Foreign Minister Roberto Conde, stated that Uruguay would never recognise the independence of Kosovo.[^cite_note-419] |
| [Uzbekistan](31853.md) | In February 2008, the [Uzbek government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uzbek_government) stated that questions of independence should be decided in the UN assembly. As for Kosovo, Uzbekistan has yet to come up with a final position.[^cite_note-420] |

##### V

| Country | Position |
| --- | --- |
| [Venezuela](32374.md) | Main article: [Kosovo–Venezuela relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kosovo%E2%80%93Venezuela_relations) In February 2008, Venezuelan president [Hugo Chávez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hugo_Ch%C3%A1vez) announced that Venezuela did not recognise Kosovo's independence on the grounds that it had been achieved through United States pressure.[^cite_note-venbol2-319] On 24 March 2008, Chávez accused [Washington](108956.md) of trying to "weaken Russia" by supporting independence for Kosovo. He called Kosovo's prime minister, [Hashim Thaçi](3626980.md), a "terrorist" put in power by the United States.[^cite_note-421] |
| [Vietnam](202354.md) | In February 2008, the Vietnamese UN Ambassador [Le Luong Minh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Le_Luong_Minh) "reaffirmed Vietnam policy that the fact that Kosovo's unilateral declaration of independence is not a correct implementation of the U.N. Security Council Resolution 1244 and that will only complicate the situation in Kosovo and the Balkan region".[^cite_note-422]In a 23 February 2011 meeting with Serbian Foreign Minister Vuk Jeremić, Vietnamese deputy prime minister, [Pham Gia Khiem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pham_Gia_Khiem), reaffirmed Vietnam's position of supporting "Kosovo-related issues under the United Nations Security Council's decree to gain comprehensive measures in terms of respecting national sovereignty and territorial integrity, and assuring the benefits of involved nations".[^cite_note-423] In a 24 November 2011 meeting with Albanian deputy prime minister and Foreign Minister, [Edmond Haxhinasto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edmond_Haxhinasto), Vietnam's Foreign Minister, [Pham Binh Minh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pham_Binh_Minh), said that Vietnam was following the developments in Kosovo, and that Serbia and Kosovo should continue the dialogue to find common ground that will be acceptable to both parties.[^cite_note-424] |

##### Z

| Country | Position |
| --- | --- |
| [Zambia](34415.md) | Main article: [Kosovo–Zambia relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kosovo%E2%80%93Zambia_relations)In March 2008, Zambian Foreign Minister, [Kabinga Pande](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kabinga_Pande), said that Zambia had not decided its position on the declaration of Kosovo's independence. Pande said the government needed more time to analyse the matter.[^cite_note-425] According to leaked US cables, Zambia did not want to take a position on Kosovo due to concerns with the precedent for the [Lozi tribe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lozi_people), an ethnic group primarily inhabiting western Zambia, which had an [active separatist movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_active_separatist_movements_in_Africa) for independence from Zambia. |
| [Zimbabwe](34399.md) | In April 2011, Claudius Nhema, Deputy Director of Protocol in the [Foreign Ministry of Zimbabwe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_\(Zimbabwe\)), reportedly told Kosovan pilot James Berisha that Zimbabwe would be considering Kosovo's recognition, but that they should wait for a recommendation from the Zimbabwean UN representative who should bring it to the Foreign Ministry after which it would be taken to [Parliament](3394393.md) for ratification.[^cite_note-426]In February 2013, the then Prime Minister of Zimbabwe, [Morgan Tsvangirai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Morgan_Tsvangirai), said that he would consider recognising Kosovo.[^cite_note-427] |

#### Other states and entities

| Country | Position |
| --- | --- |
| [Abkhazia](18933375.md) | In February 2008, [Abkhazian](18933375.md) president [Sergei Bagapsh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergei_Bagapsh), regarded "the promotion of Kosovo by the U.S.A. and some European states towards the declaration of independence as a visible demonstration of the policy of double standards". "Why does not the world community put any attention to the violent actions against ethnic minorities living in Kosovo..., the lack of interethnic reconciliation...", Bagapsh noted. "We are solidly convinced of the fact that \[now] we have got an even wider moral base for the recognition of our independence."[^cite_note-428] On 5 September 2008, the Abkhazian Foreign Minister [Sergei Shamba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergei_Shamba) said he was ready to recognise Kosovo's independence, "if Kosovo agrees to recognize our own (Abkhazia) independence, we will certainly recognize them as well".[^cite_note-429][^cite_note-430] |
| [Holy See](13393.md) | Main article: [Holy See's reaction to the 2008 Kosovo declaration of independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holy_See's_reaction_to_the_2008_Kosovo_declaration_of_independence) The Holy See does not recognise Kosovo and supports the territorial respect and integrity of [Serbia](29265.md) and per [UN 1244](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_1244), part of agreement with [Eastern Orthodox Church](10186.md), and this has led to a warming of Holy See-Serbia relations.[^cite_note-431] The [Cardinal](6221.md) [Walter Kasper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walter_Kasper), President of the [Pontifical Council for Promoting Christian Unity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pontifical_Council_for_Promoting_Christian_Unity), stated that the Holy See had not recognised the independence of Kosovo and did not intend to do so in the future.[^cite_note-432][^cite_note-433] |
| [Northern Cyprus](271409.md) | In February 2008, the President of Northern Cyprus [Mehmet Ali Talat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mehmet_Ali_Talat) welcomed Kosovo's independence, but a presidential spokesman said that the Turkish Republic of Northern Cyprus was not planning to recognise Kosovo.[^cite_note-434][^cite_note-435][^cite_note-436] |
| [Palestine](241405.md) | In February 2008, two senior Palestinian officials representing the [Mahmoud Abbas](232595.md) [West Bank](33209.md)-controlling government, who are also part of the team negotiating with Israel, disagreed on what the Kosovo events implied for Palestine. [Yasser Abed Rabbo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yasser_Abed_Rabbo) said, "If things are not going in the direction of continuous and serious negotiations, then we should take the step and announce our independence unilaterally. Kosovo is not better than us. We deserve independence even before Kosovo, and we ask for the backing of the United States and the European Union for our independence". [Saeb Erekat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saeb_Erekat) responded that the [Palestine Liberation Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestine_Liberation_Organization) had already declared independence in 1988. "Now we need real independence, not a declaration," said Erekat, "We need real independence by ending the occupation. We are not Kosovo. We are under Israeli occupation and for independence we need to acquire independence".[^cite_note-437] During a July 2009 state visit to Serbia, President of the Palestinian National Authority, Mahmoud Abbas, when discussing both the situations in the Middle East and Kosovo said, "We are looking for a way to resolve these problems in a peaceful way, by upholding international law. We cannot impose solutions nor can we accept imposed solutions. That is why we must negotiate".[^cite_note-438][^cite_note-439] In September 2011, during the meeting of Foreign Ministers of the [Non-Aligned Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-Aligned_Movement) summit in Belgrade, the Palestinian Ambassador to the UN [Riyad Mansour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Riyad_Mansour) said that Palestine was a "typical foreign occupation which cannot be compared to the issue of Kosovo" as confirmed by international law and the UN.[^cite_note-440][^cite_note-441] |
| [Sahrawi Republic](38762388.md) | The [Polisario Front](201574.md), which governs the Sahrawi Arab Democratic Republic, has stated that the speedy recognition of Kosovo's independence by many countries shows the double standards of the international community, considering that the [Western Sahara](33220.md) issue remains unsolved after three decades.[^cite_note-442] |
| [Somaliland](51425.md) | In 2010, the President of Somaliland, [Ahmed Mahamoud Silanyo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahmed_Mahamoud_Silanyo), said, "We are heartened by Kosovo and what's happened to [Southern Sudan](32350676.md) that means it opens the door for us. The principle that countries should remain as they were at the time of independence has changed so why should it not work for us as well".[^cite_note-443] |
| [South Ossetia](318381.md) | In February 2008, the South Ossetian president, [Eduard Kokoity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eduard_Kokoity), stated that it is not fair to compare this breakaway region with Kosovo because South Ossetians have far more right to a state of their own than Kosovo Albanians. He said that "Kosovo Albanians got independence after [NATO's aggression on Serbia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1999_NATO_bombing_of_Yugoslavia). Americans and NATO member countries took away Serbia's province. I feel sincerely for the Serb people," and that "Serbs had a well-organized state that provided for a normal life for Albanians. For this reason, what Americans have done to the Serbs was injustice".[^cite_note-444] |
| [Transnistria](376581.md) | Transnistria has no policy towards Kosovo, but the Transnistrian Foreign Ministry has said that "The declaration and recognition of Kosovo are of fundamental importance, since thereby a new conflict settlement model has been established, based on the priority of people's right to self-determination. Pridnestrovie \[Transnistria] holds that this model should be applicable to all conflicts which have similar political, legal, and economic bases".[^cite_note-445] |

## Positions taken by intergovernmental organisations

Main article: [Membership of Kosovo in international organizations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Membership_of_Kosovo_in_international_organizations)

[Intergovernmental organisations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intergovernmental_organisation) do not themselves diplomatically recognise any state; their member states do so individually. However, depending on the intergovernmental organisation's rules of internal governance and the positions of their member states, they may express positive or negative opinions as to declarations of independence, or choose to offer or withhold membership to a partially recognised state.

| International organisation | Position |
| --- | --- |
| [Arab League](52625.md) | In May 2009, the Secretary General of the Arab League, [Amr Moussa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amr_Moussa), welcomed a request by Kosovo's Foreign Minister, Skënder Hyseni, to establish regular communications.[^cite_note-446] At a meeting on 18 June 2009 with Hyseni, the representative of the Arab League to the UN, [Yahya Mahmassani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yahya_Mahmassani), said that the Kosovo issue was being discussed at the Arab League, and that there would be gradual movement towards recognition as most Arab states are supportive of Kosovo.[^cite_note-Bahamas_and_Mozambique-447] Member states (16 / 22)Algeria • Bahrain <sup>†</sup> • Comoros <sup>†</sup> • Djibouti <sup>†</sup> • Egypt <sup>†</sup> • Iraq • Jordan <sup>†</sup> • Kuwait <sup>†</sup> • Lebanon • Libya <sup>†</sup> • Mauritania <sup>†</sup> • Morocco • Oman <sup>†</sup> • Palestine • Qatar <sup>†</sup> • Saudi Arabia <sup>†</sup> • Somalia <sup>†</sup> • Sudan <sup>†</sup> • Syria <sup>†</sup> • Tunisia • United Arab Emirates <sup>†</sup> • Yemen <sup>†</sup>  <sup>†</sup> – Have recognised Kosovo. |
| [Caribbean Community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caribbean_Community) (CARICOM) | In August 2010, Albanian Parliament Speaker [Jozefina Topalli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jozefina_Topalli) received a letter from the chairman of the Grenadian Parliament, George J. McGuire, stating that CARICOM members would soon make a joint decision on the recognition of Kosovo.[^cite_note-Grenada_CARICOM-448] On 19 August 2011, it was reported that the CARICOM members had made a joint decision to recognise Kosovo, but that each state would announce official recognition separately.[^cite_note-449] Member states (11 / 15)Antigua and Barbuda <sup>†</sup> • Bahamas <sup>†</sup> • Barbados <sup>†</sup> • Belize <sup>†</sup> • Dominica • Grenada <sup>†</sup> • Guyana <sup>†</sup> • Haiti <sup>†</sup> • Jamaica • Montserrat <sup>‡</sup> • Saint Kitts and Nevis <sup>†</sup> • Saint Lucia <sup>†</sup> • Saint Vincent and the Grenadines • Suriname <sup>†</sup> • Trinidad and Tobago  <sup>†</sup> – Have recognised Kosovo.  <sup>‡</sup> – British Overseas Territory; the UK (which recognises Kosovo) represents its foreign affairs. |
| [Europe](9239.md) [Council of Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_of_Europe) (CoE) | Kosovo plans to apply for membership in the CoE since it considers that it fulfills the statutory requirements to do so. If Kosovo receives positive votes from 2/3 of the member countries, it will be admitted to the CoE. Kosovo has already been recognised by 2/3 of the CoE members, thus it should be able to join the organisation.[^cite_note-450][^cite_note-451][^cite_note-452]  In May 2012, the Chairman of the Committee of Ministers of the CoE, [Edmond Haxhinasto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edmond_Haxhinasto), pledged to work for a stronger role for Kosovo during the Albanian chairmanship of the Council.[^cite_note-453] Haxhinasto added that Kosovo would in the near future be a part of the family of states of the Council of Europe.[^cite_note-454] However, the Secretary-General of the CoE, [Thorbjørn Jagland](192081.md), commented that membership of Kosovo depends on the willingness of members.[^cite_note-455] The [Council of Europe Development Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_of_Europe_Development_Bank)'s board of directors voted in favour of Kosovo's membership on 14 June 2013 during their meeting in Malta.[^cite_note-456] In June 2014, Kosovo became a member state of the [Venice Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venice_Commission), an advisory body of the Council of Europe.[^cite_note-457] On 24 April 2023, the first phase of Kosovo's bid to join the Council of Europe was completed, when it was accepted by the Committee of Ministers with a two-thirds majority.[^cite_note-458] However, since May 2024, Germany (supported by France and Italy) have blocked a final vote on Kosovo's membership until the Kosovo government submits a revised draft statute of the proposed [Community of Serb Municipalities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Community_of_Serb_Municipalities) to the [Constitutional Court of Kosovo](26764195.md) for review, a step which the Kosovo government has thus far been unwilling to take.[^cite_note-459] Member states (34 / 46)Albania <sup>†</sup> • Andorra <sup>†</sup> • Armenia • Austria <sup>†</sup> • Azerbaijan • Belgium <sup>†</sup> • Bosnia and Herzegovina • Bulgaria <sup>†</sup> • Croatia <sup>†</sup> • Cyprus • Czech Republic <sup>†</sup> • Denmark <sup>†</sup> • Estonia <sup>†</sup> • Finland <sup>†</sup> • France <sup>†</sup> • Georgia • Germany Kingdom <sup>†</sup>  <sup>†</sup> – Have recognised Kosovo. |
| [European Bank for Reconstruction and Development](37963.md) (EBRD) | A resolution, agreeing to the membership of Kosovo in the EBRD, was approved by its Board of Governors on 16 November 2012, providing that, by 17 December 2012, it has completed the necessary internal procedures.[^cite_note-460][^cite_note-461] On 8 February 2013, Kosovo's Foreign Minister, Enver Hoxhaj, and the President of EBRD, [Suma Chakrabarti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suma_Chakrabarti), signed an agreement on economic co-operation and activities.[^cite_note-462] |
| [European Union](9317.md) (EU) | Main article: [Accession of Kosovo to the European Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Accession_of_Kosovo_to_the_European_Union)The EU, like other IGOs, does not possess the legal capacity to diplomatically recognise any state; member states do so individually. The majority of member states have recognised Kosovo. To articulate a common EU policy of either support or opposition to Kosovo's independence would require unanimity on the subject from all 27 member states, which does not presently exist. On 18 February 2008, the EU officially stated that it would "take note" of the resolution of the Kosovo assembly.[^cite_note-463] The EU sent a [EULEX](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Union_Rule_of_Law_Mission_in_Kosovo) mission to Kosovo, which included a special representative and 2,000 police and judicial personnel.[^cite_note-464][^cite_note-465] Although the [European Parliament](9581.md) has not been formally vested with the authority to shape the EU's foreign policy, it was seen to be expressing its acceptance of Kosovan independence when it hosted the Kosovan Assembly in an interparliamentary meeting on 30 May 2008. This was also the first time Kosovo's flag was officially hoisted at an EU institution.[^cite_note-466][^cite_note-467] On 5 February 2009, the European Parliament adopted a resolution that encouraged all EU member states to recognise Kosovo. The resolution also welcomed the successful deployment of EULEX across Kosovo, and rejected the possibility of Kosovo's partition. It was passed with 424 votes in favour and 133 against. Some Romanian and Communist representatives called for a new international conference on Kosovo's status or to allow the northern part of the country to join Serbia.[^cite_note-468][^cite_note-469] On 8 July 2010, the European Parliament adopted a resolution welcoming "the recognition by all Member States of the independence of Kosovo", and stating that EU member states should "step up their common approach towards Kosovo". The resolution rejected the possibility of a partition of Kosovo.[^cite_note-470] On 29 March 2012, the European Parliament adopted a resolution that urged the five EU member states that had not recognised Kosovo's independence to do so.[^cite_note-471] Member states (22 / 27) Candidates (4 / 5)Austria <sup>†</sup> • Belgium <sup>†</sup> • Bulgaria <sup>†</sup> • Cyprus • Croatia <sup>†</sup> • Czech Republic <sup>†</sup> • Denmark <sup>†</sup> • Estonia <sup>†</sup> • Finland <sup>†</sup> • France <sup>†</sup> • Germany <sup>†</sup> • Greece • Hungary <sup>†</sup> • Ireland <sup>†</sup> • Italy <sup>†</sup> • Latvia <sup>†</sup> • Lithuania <sup>†</sup> • Luxembourg <sup>†</sup> • Malta <sup>†</sup> • Netherlands <sup>†</sup> • Poland <sup>†</sup> • Portugal <sup>†</sup> • Romania • Slovakia • **Slovenia** <sup>†</sup> • Spain • Sweden <sup>†</sup> Candidates: Albania <sup>†</sup> • Montenegro <sup>†</sup> • North Macedonia <sup>†</sup> • Serbia • Turkey <sup>†</sup>  **Bold** – Presiding the [Council of the European Union](5866.md) at time of declaration  <sup>†</sup> – Have recognised Kosovo. |
| [International Monetary Fund](15251.md) (IMF) | On 15 July 2008, the IMF issued a statement saying "It has been determined that Kosovo has seceded from Serbia as a new independent state and that Serbia is the continuing state," thus acknowledging the separation of Kosovo from Serbia.[^cite_note-472] After their membership was approved in a secret ballot by 108 states, Kosovo signed the IMF's Articles of Agreement on 29 June 2009 to become a full member of the fund.[^cite_note-473][^cite_note-474] |
| [Interpol](14338129.md) | To become a member of Interpol, a country would need the votes of 2/3 of Interpol's 195 members.[^cite_note-475] Kosovo has tried to join [Interpol](14338129.md) on three different occasions, most recently in November 2018, when it received positive votes from 68 countries, falling short of the two-thirds majority needed.[^cite_note-476] |
| [International Organization for Migration](36893.md) (IOM) | At a meeting on 30 March 2012 with Kosovo's Deputy Foreign Minister, [Petrit Selimi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petrit_Selimi), deputy director of the IOM, Laura Thomson, expressed readiness to continue advanced discussions with the representatives of Kosovo to further advance the prospects for membership.[^cite_note-IOM_EBU-477] |
| [NATO](21133.md) [North Atlantic Treaty Organization](21133.md) (NATO) | Main article: [Kosovo–NATO relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kosovo%E2%80%93NATO_relations)NATO maintains that its ongoing [Kosovo Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kosovo_Force) mission and mandate remain unchanged and that "NATO reaffirms that KFOR shall remain in Kosovo on the basis of [UNSCR 1244](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNSCR_1244), as agreed by Foreign Ministers in December 2007, unless the UN Security Council decides otherwise".[^cite_note-478] Member states (28 / 32) Candidates (0 / 1)Albania <sup>†</sup> • Belgium <sup>†</sup> • Bulgaria <sup>†</sup> • Canada <sup>†</sup> • Croatia <sup>†</sup> • Czech Republic <sup>†</sup> • Denmark <sup>†</sup> • Estonia <sup>†</sup> • Finland <sup>†</sup> • France <sup>†</sup> • Germany <sup>†</sup> • Greece • Hungary <sup>†</sup> • Iceland <sup>†</sup> • Italy <sup>†</sup> • Latvia <sup>†</sup> • Lithuania <sup>†</sup> • Luxembourg <sup>†</sup> • Montenegro <sup>†</sup> • Netherlands <sup>†</sup> • North Macedonia <sup>†</sup> • Norway <sup>†</sup> • Poland <sup>†</sup> • Portugal <sup>†</sup> • Romania • Slovakia • Slovenia <sup>†</sup> • Spain • Sweden <sup>†</sup> • Turkey <sup>†</sup> • United Kingdom <sup>†</sup> • United States <sup>†</sup> Candidates: Bosnia and Herzegovina  <sup>†</sup> – Have recognised Kosovo. |
| [Organisation of Islamic Cooperation](47725.md) (OIC) | In February 2008, Secretary General of the OIC [Ekmeleddin İhsanoğlu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ekmeleddin_%C4%B0hsano%C4%9Flu) said "Kosovo has finally declared its independence after a long and determined struggle by its people. As we rejoice this happy result, we declare our solidarity with and support to our brothers and sisters there. The [Islamic Umma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ummah) wishes them success in their new battle awaiting them which is the building of a strong and prosperous a state capable of satisfying of its people".[^cite_note-479] The OIC did not call on its individual member states to extend recognition, as some member states, including Azerbaijan, Egypt, Indonesia and Sudan, were firmly against any issuance of such a statement.[^cite_note-480] On 25 May 2009, at the OIC's 36th session of the Council of Foreign Ministers in Damascus, the 57 member states adopted a resolution that noted Kosovo's declaration of independence, upheld the role of the UN in Kosovo, reaffirmed the strong interest of the OIC regarding Muslims in the Balkans, welcomed the co-operation of Kosovo with the OIC Economic and Financial institutions, and called on the international community to continue contributing to the fostering of Kosovo's economy.[^cite_note-481] It has been reported that an earlier draft of the resolution (tabled by Saudi Arabia) had called for recognition of Kosovo by Islamic countries, but this was rejected by some member states, including Syria, Egypt and Azerbaijan.[^cite_note-482] The OIC mechanism is similar to the one adopted by the EU which leaves it up to member states to decide.[^cite_note-483] In June 2011, the OIC adopted a resolution calling on member states to consider recognising Kosovo but once again it left the recognition issue to individual member states.[^cite_note-484] In November 2012, the OIC adopted a resolution calling on member states to consider recognising Kosovo based on their free and sovereign rights as well as on their national practice.[^cite_note-485] İhsanoğlu expressed support for strengthening the international subjectivity of the Republic of Kosovo.[^cite_note-486] In February 2013, the OIC renewed the previous resolution and urged all of its member states to recognise Kosovo.[^cite_note-487] Member states (37 / 57)Afghanistan | [Organization for Security and Co-operation in Europe](22796.md) (OSCE) | On 19 February 2008, Chairman [Ilkka Kanerva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ilkka_Kanerva) and OSCE Minorities Commissioner [Knut Vollebæk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Knut_Volleb%C3%A6k) called for Kosovo's government to vigorously implement agreed-upon frameworks regarding minorities.[^cite_note-488] Serbia has vowed to oppose OSCE membership for Kosovo and is calling for the organisation to condemn the declaration of independence.[^cite_note-489] Member states (36 / 56)Albania <sup>†</sup> • Andorra <sup>†</sup> • Armenia • Austria <sup>†</sup> • Azerbaijan • Belarus • Belgium <sup>†</sup> • Bosnia and Herzegovina • Bulgaria <sup>†</sup> • Canada <sup>†</sup> • Croatia <sup>†</sup> • Cyprus • Czech Republic <sup>†</sup> • Denmark <sup>†</sup> • Estonia <sup>†</sup> • Finland <sup>†</sup> • France <sup>†</sup> • Georgia • Germany <sup>†</sup> • Greece • Hungary <sup>†</sup> • Iceland <sup>†</sup> • Ireland <sup>†</sup> • Italy <sup>†</sup> • Kazakhstan • Kyrgyzstan • Latvia <sup>†</sup> • Liechtenstein <sup>†</sup> • Lithuania <sup>†</sup> • Luxembourg <sup>†</sup> • Malta <sup>†</sup> • Moldova • Monaco <sup>†</sup> • Montenegro <sup>†</sup> • Netherlands <sup>†</sup> • North Macedonia <sup>†</sup> • Norway <sup>†</sup> • Poland <sup>†</sup> • Portugal <sup>†</sup> • Romania • Russia • San Marino <sup>†</sup> • Serbia • Slovakia • Slovenia <sup>†</sup> • Spain • Sweden <sup>†</sup> • Switzerland <sup>†</sup> • Tajikistan • Turkey <sup>†</sup> • Turkmenistan • Ukraine • United Kingdom <sup>†</sup> • United States <sup>†</sup> • Uzbekistan • Vatican City  <sup>†</sup> – Have recognised Kosovo. |
| [United Nations](31769.md) (UN) | Russia called an emergency session of the [United Nations Security Council](31956.md) on 17 February 2008, but the council members, given differences in stated position between permanent members, failed to reach a consensus. Russia requested another meeting on 18 February. In March 2008 the [UNMIK](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNMIK) mission in Kosovo told the [Serbian government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serbian_government) to cease its interference in [North Kosovo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Kosovo) after local Serbs burned down a customs office set up by the Republic of Kosovo.[^cite_note-490] In order for Kosovo to attain a UN seat, it would require the agreement of the five permanent members of the Security Council, of which only three currently recognise Kosovo: UK, France, and the US. On 17 January 2012, the President of Kosovo, [Atifete Jahjaga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atifete_Jahjaga), had a meeting with the [president of the United Nations General Assembly](780080.md), [Nassir Abdulaziz Al-Nasser](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nassir_Abdulaziz_Al-Nasser), who stated that he will continue to support Kosovo in all initiatives and processes through which it is running.[^cite_note-491] On 11 July 2012, the elected President of the United Nations General Assembly, Serb Vuk Jeremić, said that Kosovo's move to join the UN during his upcoming presidency of the UN General Assembly would be "an act of pointless provocation". "As long as Serbia presides over the UN, and that's for the next year, this could only happen over my dead body," Jeremić said.[^cite_note-492] However, Secretary-General [Ban Ki-moon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ban_Ki-moon) said that Jeremić should have stated this as an official of Serbia, not as the President of the UN General Assembly.[^cite_note-493] **Member states (110 / 193)** Permanent members of Security Council (3 / 5)China • France <sup>†</sup> • Russia • United Kingdom <sup>†</sup> • United States <sup>†</sup>  <sup>†</sup> – Have recognised Kosovo. |
| [World Bank](36752.md) | On 29 June 2009, the Republic of Kosovo became a full member of the World Bank.[^cite_note-494] |
| [World Customs Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Customs_Organization) | On 3 March 2017, the Republic of Kosovo became a full member of the World Customs Organization.[^cite_note-495] |

## Positions taken by other actors

### Autonomous regions and secessionist movements

| Entity | Position |
| --- | --- |
| [Balochistan](8431743.md) | In August 2010, former Baloch separatist leader [Jumma Khan Marri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jumma_Khan_Marri) welcomed the independence of Kosovo and the advisory opinion by the ICJ that the declaration of independence by Kosovo was not in violation of international law.[^cite_note-496] However, [Jumma Khan Marri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jumma_Khan_Marri) has since distanced himself from the movement and now advocates against secession.[^cite_note-497] In October 2010, former Minister of Fisheries and opposition member of the Balochistan Assembly Kachkol Ali hailed the decision of the [International Court of Justice](14918.md) on Kosovo's declaration of Independence as "a glorious judgment for the national liberation movements". He said that it was a beacon of hope for enslaved nations.[^cite_note-498] |
| [Basque Government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basque_Government) | The regional Basque government, unlike the central Spanish government in [Madrid](41188263.md), responded very positively to Kosovo's declaration of independence. A regional government spokeswoman said that "It's a lesson to be followed when it comes to peaceful and democratic solutions of the identity and allegiance problems ... It shows that respect of the citizens' will is the key to solving difficult political problems".[^cite_note-499] |
| [Catalonia](6822.md) | In July 2010, following the ICJ decision, the Catalan nationalist parties expressed that there are clear parallels between their case and Kosovo's. [Joan Puigcercós](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joan_Puigcerc%C3%B3s), the President of the [Republican Left of Catalonia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republican_Left_of_Catalonia), stated that the ICJ decision shows that Catalonia's independence could be legal and recognised at an international level. The [Democratic Convergence of Catalonia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Convergence_of_Catalonia) party asked the Spanish Government to recognise Kosovo's independence and the right of self-determination of the people.[^cite_note-500] On 23 July 2010, [José Montilla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Montilla), President of the [Generalitat of Catalonia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Generalitat_of_Catalonia), said that Catalonia and Kosovo have little in common.[^cite_note-501] In March 2012 during a fierce debate with Spanish prime minister [Mariano Rajoy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mariano_Rajoy), [Convergence and Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Convergence_and_Union) general secretary [Josep Antoni Duran i Lleida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Josep_Antoni_Duran_i_Lleida) asked for the immediate recognition of Kosovo by Spain, and stated that the reasons for non-recognition "are not international but internal".[^cite_note-502] |
| [Central Tibetan Administration](54751.md) ([government in exile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_in_exile)) | In June 2008, an article was published on the website of the Central Tibetan Administration saying that if Kosovo has a right to independence then Tibet has every right to become an independent nation and Tibetans are fully entitled to the right of self-determination.[^cite_note-503] In April 2010, the [14th Dalai Lama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/14th_Dalai_Lama), then joint executive authority within the government in exile, sent a telegram of congratulations to Kosovo's prime minister, [Hashim Thaçi](3626980.md), saying that he is satisfied with the independence of Kosovo and that he prays that Kosovo's democratic state will be a model for others to follow.[^cite_note-504] |
| [Chechen Republic of Ichkeria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chechen_Republic_of_Ichkeria) ([government in exile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_in_exile)) | Usman Ferzauli, the Foreign Minister of the Chechen Republic of Ichkeria, said that his country "welcome the declaration of state independence by Kosovo and do not question the right of the people of Kosovo to distance themselves from the state that terrorized it".[^cite_note-505] The Prime Minister of the Chechen Republic of Ichkeria, [Akhmed Zakayev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Akhmed_Zakayev), stated in an April 2010 interview that to him and his nation, Kosovo represented a hope, and also made reference to a letter he had apparently sent to Kosovo's prime minister [Hashim Thaçi](3626980.md) congratulating the latter.[^cite_note-506] |
| [East Turkestan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Turkestan) ([government in exile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_in_exile)) | On 18 February 2008, Ansar Yusuf Turani, the representative of the government-in-exile, released a press statement saying "On behalf of the people of East Turkistan, the East Turkistan Government in Exile hereby recognizes Kosovo as an independent and sovereign state and wishes peace and prosperity for the people of Kosovo".[^cite_note-507] |
| [Jammu Kashmir Liberation Front](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jammu_Kashmir_Liberation_Front) | On 11 March 2008, the Jammu Kashmir Liberation Front staged a demonstration in Brussels in front of the European Commission building. It was headed by one of its leaders, Barrister Abdul Majeed Tramboo, and its agenda cited Kosovo's independence, demanding equal treatment and commensurate application of the same solution by the EU in the [Kashmir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kashmir) dispute involving India, Pakistan, and China. Protesters included EU Parliament members, students, and various NGO constituents and representatives.[^cite_note-508] |
| [Mejlis of the Crimean Tatar People](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mejlis_of_the_Crimean_Tatar_People) | [Mustafa Dzhemilev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mustafa_Dzhemilev), the Chairman of the Mejlis of the Crimean Tatar People, declared that he supported the right of self-determination for every nation, including Kosovo.[^cite_note-509] He also added that the [Crimean Tatars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crimean_Tatars) will not start a secession process from Ukraine if their rights are respected. Cemilev stated that he believes the motive for the Kosovars to declare independence was the anti-Albanian situation in Kosovo.[^cite_note-510] |

### International non-governmental organisations

See also: [Membership of Kosovo in international sports federations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Membership_of_Kosovo_in_international_sports_federations)

| International organisation | Position |
| --- | --- |
| [European Broadcasting Union](10380.md) (EBU) | [Radio Television of Kosovo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radio_Television_of_Kosovo) (RTK) is not an active member of the EBU, and therefore, they cannot participate in the [Eurovision Song Contest](9954.md) and sister projects. However, there is a co-operation agreement between RTK and the EBU[^cite_note-511] and they were allowed to participate in the [Eurovision Young Dancers 2011](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurovision_Young_Dancers_2011) competition. On 30 March 2012, during a meeting in Geneva with Kosovo's Deputy Foreign Minister [Petrit Selimi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petrit_Selimi), Ingrid Delterne, executive director of the EBU, expressed readiness for Kosovo's membership in the [ITU](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Telecommunication_Union).[^cite_note-IOM_EBU-477] |
| [International Olympic Committee](15147.md) (IOC) | The [Olympic Committee of Kosovo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olympic_Committee_of_Kosovo) became a full member of the International Olympic Committee on 9 December 2014.[^cite_note-512] The Olympic Committee of Kosovo has been in existence since 1992.[^cite_note-513] Kosovo was a provisional member of the IOC from 22 October 2014 through 9 December 2014.[^cite_note-514] |
| [Fédération Internationale de Football Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F%C3%A9d%C3%A9ration_Internationale_de_Football_Association) (FIFA) | Kosovo played their first official match in 2014, against Haiti. In April 2016, Kosovo was voted into UEFA, and on 13 May 2016, at the 66th FIFA congress in Mexico City, Kosovo (along with Gibraltar) was voted into the organisation. Only 23 associations voted against Kosovo's membership. They took part in their first World Cup qualifier in their 1–1 draw with Finland. |
| [Unrepresented Nations and Peoples Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unrepresented_Nations_and_Peoples_Organization) (UNPO) | UNPO issued a statement on 18 February 2008: "for regions in similar conditions, Kosova's independence represents new hope for the future of their own potential statehood".[^cite_note-515] In the days that followed, several African UNPO members expressed their own individual secession-minded reactions to Kosovo's independence.[^cite_note-516] |
| [Norwegian Nobel Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norwegian_Nobel_Committee) | After former President of Finland [Martti Ahtisaari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martti_Ahtisaari) received the [2008 Nobel Peace Prize](26230922.md) "for his important efforts ... to resolve international conflicts",[^cite_note-517] including his work in Kosovo as a UN special envoy, the [Norwegian Nobel Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norwegian_Nobel_Committee) Secretary, who had been also the Director of the Norwegian Nobel Institute, [Geir Lundestad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geir_Lundestad), said that the committee believed "there is no alternative to an independent Kosovo".[^cite_note-518] |
| [International Organization for Standardization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Organization_for_Standardization) (ISO) | Kosovo is not a member of the governing structures for the ISO. Independently of its ISO membership status, ISO will also potentially issue a standardised country code for Kosovo. According to rules of procedure followed by the [ISO 3166](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ISO_3166) Maintenance Agency based in Geneva, a new [ISO 3166-1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ISO_3166-1) code for Kosovo will only be issued once it appears in the UN Terminology Bulletin *Country Names* or in the UN Statistics Division's list of Country and Region Codes for Statistical Use.[^cite_note-519] To appear in the terminology bulletin, it must either (a) be admitted into the UN, (b) join a [UN Specialised Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_specialized_agencies_of_the_United_Nations) or (c) become a state party to the *Statute of the [International Court of Justice](14918.md)*.[^cite_note-520] Criterion (b) was met when Kosovo joined the International Monetary Fund and World Bank; a terminology bulletin has yet to be circulated. |
| [Internet Corporation for Assigned Names and Numbers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internet_Corporation_for_Assigned_Names_and_Numbers) (ICANN) | ICANN, through its [Country Code Names Supporting Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Country_Code_Names_Supporting_Organization), is responsible for adding new [country code top-level domains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Country_code_top-level_domain) (ccTLDs) for use in Internet addressing. Rules of procedure dictate Kosovo must first receive an [ISO 3166-1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ISO_3166-1) code (discussed above) before the ccTLD can be introduced; speculation has centred on ".ks" as the likeliest candidate.[^cite_note-521][^cite_note-522] |
| [International Road and Transport Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Road_and_Transport_Union_\(IRU\)) (IRU) | Kosovo officially became the 181st member of the IRU in May 2009.[^cite_note-523] |
| [International Bar Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Bar_Association) (IBA) | Kosovo officially became a member of the IBA on 28 May 2009.[^cite_note-524][^cite_note-525] |
